On 3 January 2026, the [United States](3434750.md) launched a [military strike](897238.md) in [Venezuela](32374.md) and captured incumbent Venezuelan president [Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) and his wife, [Cilia Flores](9530834.md).[^cite_note-Cano-2026-15][^cite_note-16] The US operation, codenamed **Operation Absolute Resolve**,[^cite_note-Caine-2026-17][^cite_note-18] began around 2 a.m. local time, when explosions were observed.[^cite_note-NTN24-19] The [US Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Armed_Forces) bombed infrastructure across northern Venezuela to [suppress air defenses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suppression_of_enemy_air_defenses) as an apprehension force attacked Maduro's compound in [Caracas](55289.md).[^cite_note-NYTStrike-20][^cite_note-Peniston-2026-21] Approximately 80 people, including Venezuelan and Cuban military and civilians, died and seven American soldiers were wounded. Maduro and Flores were transported to New York City by US forces[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23] and were [indicted on drug trafficking charges](82024138.md)[^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25] to which Maduro and Flores pleaded not guilty.[^cite_note-26][^cite_note-27]

US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) and [his administration](78298108.md) justified the operation as a law-enforcement action with military support, saying that the president has "inherent constitutional authority" to undertake such an act.[^cite_note-28] Venezuelan vice president [Delcy Rodríguez](44199228.md) denounced Maduro's "[kidnapping](169376.md)".[^cite_note-Tiago-2026-29][^cite_note-30] Venezuelan officials said at least 23[^cite_note-casualties-11] Venezuelan security officers were killed during the attack.[^cite_note-Independent6Jan-13][^cite_note-AP24kills-31] The [Cuban](5042481.md) government said that 32 members of the Cuban military and intelligence agencies were killed.[^cite_note-NYT_Live_260104-35][^cite_note-AJ04-12] Officials in the [United Nations](31769.md) (UN), the US, and other countries,[^cite_note-36][^cite_note-37] as well as international law experts said the raid violated the [UN Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Charter) and Venezuela's sovereignty.[^cite_note-CBS_News-2026-38][^cite_note-NYTStrike-20][^cite_note-McKelvie_Guardian-39][^cite_note-auto-40] Other reactions around the world included celebrations by the [Venezuelan diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_diaspora) and protests against the attack.[^cite_note-Macaya-2026-41][^cite_note-wpix-3jan2026-42]

The Venezuelan government remained in place, with Rodríguez sworn in as acting president on 5 January 2026.[^cite_note-Reuters2026a-43] Multiple political prisoners (including foreigners) detained in Venezuela [were released](82304479.md),[^cite_note-Reuters2026b-44] a gesture which, according to Trump, contributed to avoiding a second wave of attacks.[^cite_note-reuterscancelwave2-45] On 30 January, Rodríguez announced an amnesty bill for political prisoners covering the period of 1999 to present,[^cite_note-announcementamnesty-46] which was approved on 19 February.[^cite_note-amnestybillAPconfirmed-47] As of 8 March, the number of political prisoners released confirmed since 8 January was 621 out of an estimate of over 800 held before January, according to human rights organizations.[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-amnestybillAPconfirmed-47][^cite_note-delcyspeechprisoners-49] By February, the US and Venezuela had restarted diplomatic relations, with the [Embassy of the United States, Caracas](48416680.md), being reopened for the first time since 2019.[^cite_note-\:3-50] According to *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, Venezuela has been a *de facto* [puppet state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puppet_state) since the intervention, with the US State Department under [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) exercising control over core aspects of Venezuela's governance, including its domestic finances, government appointments, revenues, foreign policy, and the distribution of its natural resources.[^cite_note-Callaghan_01042026-9][^cite_note-NYT_07112026-10][^cite_note-\:6-51]

Trump and his administration made clear that [access to Venezuelan oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_involvement_in_Venezuela's_petroleum_industry) was a core reason for the action.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_oil-52] The US announced a 50-million-barrel oil supply deal with the remaining government in Venezuela, with the first \$300 million already received on 20 January.[^cite_note-first300M-53] On 29 January, a new law was passed by Rodríguez to give private companies control over the production and sale of oil.[^cite_note-signlaw-54] In parallel, the US lifted sanctions imposed on Venezuelan oil trade and issued licenses for companies to trade Venezuelan oil.[^cite_note-firsteasesanctions-55][^cite_note-reuterssanctions2licences-56] Since the attack, the Trump Administration has collected more than \$13 billion in proceeds from Venezuelan oil sales, without financial oversight. It has used the disbursement of oil revenue as leverage against Rodríguez's government.[^cite_note-57]

## Background

See also: [Proposed United States invasion of Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proposed_United_States_invasion_of_Venezuela)

### Political, economic and international

See also: [Sanctions during the Venezuelan crisis](60408367.md)

[Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) has been criticized for authoritarian rule since [Hugo Chávez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Ch%C3%A1vez) hand-picked him as the next [president of Venezuela](3329692.md) in 2013.[^cite_note-ReutersMaduroGrip-58] According to [Reuters](18998750.md), "Maduro ruled Venezuela with a heavy hand ... presiding over deep economic and social crises and resisting pressure from domestic opponents and foreign governments for political change", and was "long accused by critics both at home and abroad of being a dictator who jailed or persecuted political opponents and repeatedly staged sham elections".[^cite_note-ReutersMaduroGrip-58] [Mark Weisbrot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Weisbrot) states that Venezuela has faced an [economic crisis](52966258.md) since the [oil glut of the 2010s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010s_oil_glut), due to its export-dependent economy.[^cite_note-59]

For years, the US government pressured Maduro, considering him an "illegitimate leader".[^cite_note-60] Unilateral US sanctions on Venezuela began in the 2010s, and escalated through 2025. According to a 2019 report by [Mark Weisbrot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Weisbrot) and [Jeffrey Sachs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Sachs), sanctions caused Venezuela a loss of \$38 billion between 2016 and 2019, prevented the government from resolving the crisis using fiscal and monetary policy changes, and in combination with preexisting negative trends resulted in an estimated 40,000 excess deaths between 2017 and 2018.[^cite_note-Weisbrot-2019-61] The report's methodology and conclusions were criticized by other economists.[^cite_note-62][^cite_note-63]

Maduro's victory in the [2018 Venezuelan presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Venezuelan_presidential_election) was disputed and 45 countries did not accept the results, causing a [presidential crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_presidential_crisis) that divided the international community from 2019 to 2023.[^cite_note-64] In May 2020, Venezuelan dissidents backed by [Silvercorp USA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Goudreau#Silvercorp_USA) attempted to infiltrate Venezuela by sea and overthrow Maduro in [Operation Gideon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Gideon_\(2020\)). The raid failed, and the dissidents and mercenaries were killed or captured.[^cite_note-65] The owner of Silvercorp, an ex-US special operations sergeant, said he met with Trump administration officials about the plan.[^cite_note-66]

Absent any vote tallies, Maduro was declared the winner of the [2024 Venezuelan presidential election](60825396.md) by the government-controlled [National Electoral Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Electoral_Council_\(Venezuela\)); the result was "widely condemned as fraudulent by international observers and the opposition"[^cite_note-ReutersMaduroGrip-58] who found statistical improbabilities in the results.[^cite_note-ReutersMaduroGrip-58][^cite_note-67][^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69] The opposition showed evidence and vote tallies that its candidate, [Edmundo González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmundo_Gonz%C3%A1lez), had won a majority, matching the results of pre-election opinion polls,[^cite_note-BBC_2026-70][^cite_note-Wells-Mackintosh-71] who was "widely believed to have won \[the] election, and by a landslide", according to *The New York Times*.[^cite_note-NYTRecognized-72] The Venezuelan government issued an arrest warrant for González, who left the country and was granted asylum in Spain.[^cite_note-73] In January 2025, the [Biden administration](65732174.md) recognized González as Venezuela's legitimate president-elect.[^cite_note-NYTRecognized-72] The Trump administration had sought to prosecute Maduro on narco-terrorism charges since at least 2020.[^cite_note-74] At a 2023 press conference, Trump said, "Venezuela was about to collapse. We would have taken over it... \[and] kept all that oil."[^cite_note-75]

### Oil and mineral resources

See also: [History of U.S. involvement in Venezuela's petroleum industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_U.S._involvement_in_Venezuela's_petroleum_industry), [History of the Venezuelan oil industry](16852238.md), [Oil reserves in Venezuela](19100139.md), and [Oil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_war)



Venezuela has the [largest proven crude oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_proven_oil_reserves) of any country in the world.

[^cite_note-76]

[^cite_note-SciAm_20260107-77]

Venezuela holds the world's largest proven [oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_reserves), estimated at 17% of the global total, or 300 billion barrels.[^cite_note-nyt20251205-78] In December 2025, the US began [a maritime blockade](81814844.md) of sanctioned oil or [shadow fleet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shadow_fleet) tankers.[^cite_note-79]

The 2025–2026 operations are seen by the Trump administration as serving the triple goals of crippling Maduro, disrupting drug trade routes, and getting access to Venezuelan oil; Trump stated that revenue generated would go to the Venezuelan people, US oil companies, and "to the United States of America in the form of reimbursement for the damages caused us by that country".[^cite_note-nyt202601Troianovski-80] Following the raid, Trump's press conference made clear that oil was a motivation for action in Venezuela.[^cite_note-Venezuelan_oil-52] Citing the restructuring of Venezuela's oil industry in 1976 and 2007, Trump, [Stephen Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Miller), and others claimed that Venezuela had stolen oil from US companies.[^cite_note-84][^cite_note-85][^cite_note-TrumpOilClaimsWaPo-86][^cite_note-TrumpOilClaimNYT-87] Trump suggested on 5 January that seized oil could be treated as a US asset, asserting the willingness to sell it or add it to the US [Strategic Petroleum Reserve](167198.md).[^cite_note-88] On 9 January, after other sanctioned vessels were seized, and a tanker was returned to Venezuela as part of a joint operation between interim authorities in Venezuela and the US, Trump said: "This tanker is now on its way back to Venezuela, and the oil will be sold through the GREAT Energy Deal, which we have created for such sales."[^cite_note-ElPitazo10Jan-89][^cite_note-NYT9Jan-90]

The Trump administration also expressed interest in Venezuela's large deposits of [gold](12240.md) and [rare earth minerals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Critical_minerals). Some experts noted that the Maduro government had previously attempted to offer the US access to these minerals in a failed attempt to stave off military action, with the ongoing operations marking a return to "resource imperialism".[^cite_note-Hennigan-83]

Trump stated on the day of the military operation that [US oil companies](19086358.md) would invest billions to increase oil production in the country;[^cite_note-91] [ExxonMobil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ExxonMobil), [ConocoPhillips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ConocoPhillips), and [Chevron Corporation](284749.md) (the [US oil majors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Oil)) reportedly had no prior knowledge of the operation to remove Maduro and unnamed sources said they had no discussions with the Trump administration before the operation about investing in the country if Maduro was removed.[^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93] Trump further stated that US oil companies would invest \$100 billion in Venezuelan oil production;[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95] in a meeting between Trump and two dozen oil company executives on 9 January, ExxonMobil CEO [Darren Woods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darren_Woods) stated that Venezuela was "uninvestable" due to the legal framework in the country while the majority of the executives in attendance did not make firm investment commitments.[^cite_note-96][^cite_note-Reuters_1-9-2026-97] ExxonMobil and ConocoPhillips left Venezuela after [their assets were expropriated in 2007](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_U.S._involvement_in_Venezuela's_petroleum_industry#2007_Expropriations), while Chevron continued its operations.[^cite_note-Reuters_1-9-2026-97] Two days after the meeting, Trump told reporters that he was "inclined" to keep ExxonMobil out of Venezuela because he "didn't like Exxon's response" to his proposed investments for the country and thought that "They're playing too cute."[^cite_note-98]

Unlike Congress, Trump claimed to have spoken with oil companies both "before and after".[^cite_note-99][^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101]

### Indictment of Maduro

Main article: [Prosecution of Nicolás Maduro and Cilia Flores](82024138.md)

See also: [Cocaine § United States](7701.md#United_States), [Illegal drug trade in Venezuela](28018284.md), and [United States strikes on alleged drug traffickers during Operation Southern Spear](80956668.md)

US attorney general [Pam Bondi](4550623.md)'s August 2025 announcement that the reward for Maduro's arrest has increased to \$50 million

The United States accused Maduro of [electoral fraud](382165.md) and presiding over a "[narco-state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Narco-state)".[^cite_note-Cawthorne2026-102] In March 2020, during Trump's first term, the United States Justice Department [indicted Maduro on cocaine-trafficking conspiracy charges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_of_America_v._Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro), which Maduro denied.[^cite_note-Leal-2026-103] Trump designated two Venezuelan drug gangs, the [Tren de Aragua](70859925.md) and the [Cartel of the Suns](45272133.md), as [Foreign Terrorist Organizations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Terrorist_Organizations) (FTOs) in January and July 2025 respectively, and alleged that the latter was led by Maduro. Maduro denied being involved in cartel leadership and characterized the designations as an attempt to use the [war on drugs](1181646.md) for regime change.[^cite_note-BBC_2026-70][^cite_note-Wells-Mackintosh-71][^cite_note-Leal-2026-103][^cite_note-axios-104] Some Latin American crime experts have described the Cartel of the Suns as more of a general network of Venezuelan government and military corruption than as an organized group. Also in July 2025, a secret order instructed the US military to use force against cartels listed as terrorist organizations.[^cite_note-105][^cite_note-Leal-2026-103] In August 2025, the reward for Maduro's arrest increased to \$50 million with US attorney general [Pam Bondi](4550623.md) calling Maduro "one of the largest narco-traffickers in the world and a threat to our national security."[^cite_note-106]

However, the [Associated Press](18935732.md) reported in January 2026 that while the US government has never publicly accused Delcy Rodríguez of criminal wrongdoing related to the drug trade, the Drug Enforcement Administration has nonetheless gathered an extensive intelligence file on Rodríguez since 2018 and designated her as a "priority target" in 2022—which the agency does for individuals suspected of having "significant impact" on the drug trade.[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108] In testimony before the [US Senate Foreign Relations Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Senate_Committee_on_Foreign_Relations) on 28 January, US Secretary of State [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) did not confirm the story when asked by Senator [Jeanne Shaheen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeanne_Shaheen) and instead only noted that Rodríguez had not been indicted, saying: "We are dealing with individuals that in our system would not be acceptable in the long term. But we are in a transition to stabilization phase. You have to work with the people currently in charge of the elements of government."[^cite_note-109]

### United States military buildup

Main article: [United States military buildup in the Caribbean during Operation Southern Spear](80987786.md)

See also: [United States strikes on alleged drug traffickers during Operation Southern Spear § Land targets in Venezuela](80956668.md#Land_targets_in_Venezuela)

In August 2025, the United States began a military buildup in the [southern Caribbean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Caribbean), deploying warships and military personnel.[^cite_note-110][^cite_note-111] The [Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md) (CIA) sent a team into Venezuela that began tracking Maduro's movements,[^cite_note-NicholasKube-112] using a source close to the Venezuelan president, and plans were made for the capture of Maduro months in advance. The [Delta Force](328809.md) and other participants in the operation erected a [mockup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mockup) of the [safe house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safe_house) in which Maduro was believed to be hiding, described by Trump as a "very highly guarded ... fortress".[^cite_note-MockHouse-6] In September, the [United States Southern Command](1590932.md) began conducting military strikes on vessels in the [Caribbean Sea](7822.md), alleging some were [trafficking drugs](60850852.md) on behalf of Venezuela.[^cite_note-APReporterFound-113][^cite_note-APTruthNuanced-114][^cite_note-CNN15Sep-115]

In November 2025, Venezuelan opposition leader [María Corina Machado](5791389.md) promised to open Venezuela's oil and gas reserves during a business meeting in Miami attended by Trump, and the Trump administration engaged in secret talks with Maduro's government about its oil reserves.[^cite_note-Wong2025-116] In December 2025, this escalated to include [seizures of sanctioned oil tankers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_blockade_during_Operation_Southern_Spear) carrying Venezuelan crude.[^cite_note-Wong2025-116][^cite_note-Reuters20251224-117][^cite_note-NYT20251222-118] These seizures formed part of the broader blockade on sanctioned oil preceding the January 2026 operation.[^cite_note-NYT20251222-118][^cite_note-119] The US also launched [covert operations](613453.md) in Venezuela under the directive of the CIA in late December.[^cite_note-120] On 2 January 2026, Trump announced that the US military would conduct strikes inside Venezuela.[^cite_note-121]

In the weeks preceding the operation, [Trinidad and Tobago](3565457.md) signed an agreement with the US to allow US military access to its airports.[^cite_note-122] General [Dan Caine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Caine), Chairman of the Joint Chiefs of Staff, said in a 3 January press conference that the US military had spent months planning and rehearsing the operation, with forces positioned and ready by early December.[^cite_note-Peniston-2026-21] In late December, the US escalated its military efforts against Venezuela onto land, including striking a remote northern port reportedly used by Tren de Aragua for smuggling. At that time, Maduro rejected an offer from the US to go into exile in Turkey. According to *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, Maduro's public displays downplaying the seriousness of the American threats in the weeks before the operation, such as through public dancing on a stage to [electronic music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_music) sampling one of his speeches in which Maduro said "No crazy war", were a factor in Trump deciding to begin the operation.[^cite_note-Kurmanaev2026-123]

President Trump stated that the operation had been ready to launch several days earlier but was postponed due to poor weather and [cloud cover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloud_cover), which made flying into Caracas hazardous.[^cite_note-124] General Caine, added that US forces waited for conditions to improve to minimize the risk to civilian populations and explained that the mission proceeded only after the weather "broke just enough" to allow aircraft to maneuver safely into position.[^cite_note-NBC_Live-125]

### Discussions with other members of Maduro's government

According to anonymous sources, [Reuters](18998750.md) reported that United States officials established conversations with Venezuelan minister of interior [Diosdado Cabello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diosdado_Cabello) months before, in order to avoid using the security services or militant ruling-party supporters to target the Venezuelan opposition in case of an attack.[^cite_note-126][^cite_note-guardianexclusive-127]

According to the *[Miami Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miami_Herald)*, a circle of Venezuelan officials centered on [Delcy Rodríguez](44199228.md) and her brother, [Jorge](8767262.md), privately outlined a roadmap for post-Maduro Venezuela, in which Delcy would act as figurehead and exiled general [Miguel Rodríguez Torres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miguel_Rodr%C3%ADguez_Torres) (no relationship to the Rodriguez siblings) would lead a "transitional government", dubbed "Madurismo without Maduro"; in return, Venezuela would welcome US investors and gradually loosen ties to US rivals such as Iran and Russia. The Venezuelan proposals were made to US envoy [Richard Grenell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Grenell) through Qatari channels; the Trump administration reportedly rejected the overtures.[^cite_note-RodriChannel-128] According to the *[Financial Times](136566.md)*, Delcy Rodríguez's brother [Jorge Rodríguez](8767262.md) had held talks with the United States government in 2025 to have his sister lead a post-Maduro transitional government, though those talks had included Maduro being allowed to go into exile instead of being captured.[^cite_note-129] Anonymous sources speaking for *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* said that Delcy and Jorge Rodríguez were communicating with the American and [Qatar](8238258.md) officials.[^cite_note-guardianexclusive-127] According to American officials she told them in December that "'Maduro needs to go" and stating "I'll work with whatever is the aftermath.'"[^cite_note-guardianexclusive-127]

## Strikes



Map of the US strikes on Venezuela

According to US [Joint Chiefs of Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Chiefs_of_Staff) Chairman Gen [Dan Caine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Caine), Trump gave the order to proceed at 23\:46 [VET](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuela_Standard_Time) (22\:46 EST) on 2 January.[^cite_note-Peniston-2026-21][^cite_note-130][^cite_note-BBC-131][^cite_note-132] The administration did not notify the [United States Congress](31756.md) in advance of the strikes, saying they had concerns that this could endanger the mission.[^cite_note-ABC_News:_Republicans_largely_back_Trump-133][^cite_note-134] According to [Semafor](72046207.md), *[The New York Times](30680.md)* and *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* knew about the strikes before they were carried out.[^cite_note-PBSthank-135] At least seven explosions were reported at around 02\:00 VET (01\:00 EST) on 3 January, and low-flying aircraft were seen,[^cite_note-Cano_2026-136] predominantly in [La Guaira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Guaira), [Higuerote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higuerote), Meseta de Mamo, [Baruta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baruta_Municipality), [El Hatillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Hatillo_Municipality), [Charallave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charallave), and [Carmen de Uria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmen_de_Uria), most of which are in or near the capital city [Caracas](55289.md).[^cite_note-NTN24-19]







**Clockwise from top**:&#x20;

- President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) (*right*) during Operation Absolute Resolve, alongside CIA Director [John Ratcliffe](42699041.md) (*left*)

- [Joint Chiefs of Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Chiefs_of_Staff) Chairman Gen [Dan Caine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Caine) (*left*), alongside [Secretary of Defense](43998.md) [Pete Hegseth](34076202.md) (*right*)

- Trump (*left*), alongside [Secretary of State](32293.md) [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) (*center*) and [Deputy Chief of Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House_Deputy_Chief_of_Staff) [Stephen Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Miller) (*right*)

The operation involved 150 aircraft from the [United States Navy](20518076.md), [Air Force](32090.md), and [Marine Corps](17349325.md).[^cite_note-NYTStrike-20][^cite_note-TaskAndPurpose03012026-2] They included airplanes ([Lockheed Martin F-22A Raptor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_F-22_Raptor),[^cite_note-Trevithick2026-137] [Lockheed Martin F-35A/C Lightning II](11812.md),[^cite_note-Trevithick2026-137] [Boeing F/A-18E/F Super Hornet](845012.md),[^cite_note-Trevithick2026-137] [Boeing EA-18G Growler](833647.md),[^cite_note-Trevithick2026-137] [Grumman E-2D Hawkeye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grumman_E-2_Hawkeye),[^cite_note-Newdick2026-138] [Rockwell B-1B Lancer](4952.md)[^cite_note-Newdick2026-138]) and helicopters ([Boeing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_CH-47_Chinook) [MH-47G Chinooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boeing_CH-47_Chinook#MH-47G) and [MH-60M DAP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sikorsky_UH-60_Black_Hawk#Special_purpose)[^cite_note-Rogoway2026-139]), [refueling](237949.md) tankers, [electronic-warfare (EW)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic-warfare_aircraft) planes,[^cite_note-MockHouse-6] other support aircraft, and numerous [unmanned aerial vehicles](58900.md) (including the [Lockheed Martin RQ-170 Sentinel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin_RQ-170_Sentinel)).[^cite_note-Greet-140][^cite_note-Trevithick2026-137] Trump said the United States "had a fighter jet for every possible situation".[^cite_note-MockHouse-6]

Following the operation, White House press secretary [Karoline Leavitt](71739774.md) shared an eyewitness account of Venezuelan military personnel suffering injuries consistent with [sonic weaponry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonic_weapon) during the American strike.[^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142] Trump later told [Katie Pavlich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katie_Pavlich) that "we have weapons nobody else knows about \[...] we have some amazing weapons. That was an amazing attack."[^cite_note-143] In a separate interview with the *[New York Post](102227.md)*, he referred to one of these as a secret weapon called a "discombobulator," saying it caused Venezuelan defense systems, including Russian and Chinese rockets, to "not work," and that forces "pressed buttons and nothing worked."[^cite_note-144] While Trump suggested the device disrupted equipment, [CNN](62028.md) noted that reports of personnel injuries may have involved other technologies, such as acoustic or non-lethal systems such as the [Active Denial System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Active_Denial_System), rather than the classified weapon he described.[^cite_note-145]

The operation reportedly used [one-way attack drones](53703349.md), which is believed to be the first such operational usage by the US military.[^cite_note-146] Most of the explosions targeted antennas and active military bases.[^cite_note-NTN24-2026-147] Early reports suggested that the explosions were at [Generalissimo Francisco de Miranda Air Base](6558466.md) (La Carlota) and [Fort Tiuna](61790625.md), two military installations in Caracas.[^cite_note-mh-init-148] Large explosions were also reported at [Higuerote Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higuerote_Airport), on the Venezuelan coast.[^cite_note-NTN24-2026-147][^cite_note-149] Helicopters from the [160th Special Operations Aviation Regiment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/160th_Special_Operations_Aviation_Regiment) carried Delta Force soldiers into Caracas with air cover provided by the fighters and bombers. One helicopter was damaged by Venezuelan ground defenses but continued on its mission.[^cite_note-TaskAndPurpose03012026-2] [Federal Bureau of Investigation](11127.md) (FBI) personnel, including the [Hostage Rescue Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hostage_Rescue_Team), accompanied the military to formally arrest Maduro at his compound.[^cite_note-ap5jan-7][^cite_note-NYT3Jan-8][^cite_note-150]

The strikes lasted half an hour. Smoke was seen rising from one military hangar in a Caracas base, while another was left unpowered.[^cite_note-Cano_2026-136] The strikes happened under the cover of darkness as a result of both the timing of the operation and the US military's disruption of power in the city of Caracas.[^cite_note-151] Multiple shipping containers were destroyed and damaged in the La Guaira port strikes.[^cite_note-CNN-2026-152] [Satellite](27683.md) imagery from [Vantor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vantor_\(company\)) showed at least five destroyed warehouses, burned vehicles, and a blown-up security post.[^cite_note-153][^cite_note-NBC_Live-125] An aircraft, believed to be a [Beechcraft Baron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beechcraft_Baron), and a [Buk-M2E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buk_missile_system) [surface-to-air missile](182664.md) system were destroyed at Higuerote Airport.[^cite_note-154] The Venezuelan Science and Technology Minister [Gabriela Jiménez Ramírez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriela_Jim%C3%A9nez_Ram%C3%ADrez) shared footage on social media of crumbled buildings of the [Venezuelan Institute for Scientific Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_Institute_for_Scientific_Research) (IVIC) in La Guaira which she attributed to strikes of [AGM-154 glide bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AGM-154_Joint_Standoff_Weapon) by the United States.[^cite_note-155] The Venezuelan government said that Maduro declared a national [state of emergency](298608.md) after the explosions began.[^cite_note-Hawke_2026-156] The Venezuelan government promised to defend against US military actions which it accused of seeking regime change. It described them as "[imperialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_imperialism) aggression" and called for the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) (UNSC) to be convened.[^cite_note-DiazWalsh030126-157]

## Capture of Maduro

| This article is part of [a series](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro) about[Nicolás Maduro](7161476.md) |
| --- |
| Personal [Early life](7161476.md#Early_and_personal_life) [Early career](7161476.md#Early_career) [Madurismo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madurismo) [Cilia Flores](9530834.md) (wife) [Nicolás Maduro Guerra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro_Guerra) (son)   [53rd President of Venezuela](51287559.md) [Venezuelan crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_crisis) [Economic policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_policy_of_the_Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro_administration) [International trips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_international_presidential_trips_made_by_Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro) [2018 Attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Caracas_drone_attack) [Human rights](52966258.md#Human_rights) [Presidential crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_presidential_crisis) [2017 Constituent Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Constituent_National_Assembly_of_Venezuela) [Operation Tun Tun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Tun_Tun) [COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Venezuela) [Protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protests_against_Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro_and_Delcy_Rodr%C3%ADguez) [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Venezuelan_protests) [2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Venezuelan_protests) [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Venezuelan_protests) [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Venezuelan_protests)   Elections [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Venezuelan_presidential_election) [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Venezuelan_presidential_election) [2024](60825396.md)   [Deposition and prosecution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Absolute_Resolve) [2026 US intervention](82021123.md) [US trial and prosecution](82024138.md) [Cartel of the Suns allegations](45272133.md) [Tren de Aragua ties allegations](70859925.md#United_States)   [Nicolás Maduro's signature](7161476.md)  Seal of the President of Venezuela |
| [v](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro_series)[t](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template_talk\:Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro_series)[e](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Template\:Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro_series) |

Message aired in [Venezuelan television channels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_in_Venezuela) during the operation leading to Nicolas Maduro being captured.

At 05\:21 [Venezuelan Standard Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_Standard_Time) (VET) or 04\:21 [Eastern Standard Time](496028.md) (ET),[^cite_note-Trump-2026-158][^cite_note-159] Trump announced that Maduro and Flores had been captured and flown out of the country.[^cite_note-162] The capture was undertaken by the [US Army](32087.md)'s [Delta Force](328809.md),[^cite_note-TaskAndPurpose03012026-2] with on-the-ground intelligence provided by the [CIA](5183633.md).[^cite_note-MockHouse-6] Trump posted a photograph on his Truth Social account of Maduro on board [USS *Iwo Jima*](583918.md),[^cite_note-163] showing him blindfolded, with soundproof headphones and a gray [Nike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nike,_Inc.) sweatsuit, holding a plastic water bottle.[^cite_note-164] According to US Secretary of State [Marco Rubio](5502549.md), Maduro was "arrested" and would face criminal charges in the US.[^cite_note-CNN-2026-152]

[Nahum Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahum_Fern%C3%A1ndez?action=edit\&redlink=1), leader of the ruling [United Socialist Party of Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Socialist_Party_of_Venezuela), said Maduro and Flores were captured at [Fort Tiuna](61790625.md);[^cite_note-ap-whatweknow-165] they reportedly slept at multiple locations[^cite_note-NYT3Jan-8] and had a "fortress-like" compound at Fort Tiuna.[^cite_note-Newdick2026-138] Delcy Rodríguez confirmed that both Maduro and Flores were missing and demanded confirmation they were alive via an audio message on [state television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezolana_de_Televisi%C3%B3n).[^cite_note-BBC-131][^cite_note-166] According to [Reuters](18998750.md) sources, Rodríguez was in Russia on 3 January 2026.[^cite_note-167] While other rumors indicate she was vacationing in [Margarita Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margarita_Island).[^cite_note-guardianexclusive-127] Other reports indicated that Rodríguez was in Caracas.[^cite_note-168]

Shortly before 18\:00 VET (17\:00 EST), the airplane carrying Maduro and Flores landed at [Stewart Air National Guard Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stewart_Air_National_Guard_Base) in New York.[^cite_note-169] He was seen walking off the jet, surrounded by federal agents, before entering a hangar. He was then flown by helicopter to the [Westside Heliport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_30th_Street_Heliport) in [Manhattan](45470.md), New York City and then taken by an armored convoy to a local [Drug Enforcement Administration](146720.md) (DEA) field office where he was processed before ultimately being held in the [Metropolitan Detention Center Brooklyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Detention_Center,_Brooklyn).[^cite_note-NBC_Live-125][^cite_note-170]

### Legal proceedings

Main article: [Prosecution of Nicolás Maduro and Cilia Flores](82024138.md)

See also: [Transnational crime](6760775.md)

Hours after the capture, the United States attorney general [Pam Bondi](4550623.md) announced that Maduro and Flores had been indicted in the Southern District of New York on charges related to "narcoterrorism".[^cite_note-NarcoTerr-171] The indictment listed the charges as narco-terrorism [conspiracy](3406102.md#United_States), [cocaine](7701.md) importation conspiracy, possession of [machine guns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machine_gun) and destructive devices, and conspiracy to possess machine guns and destructive devices.[^cite_note-NarcoTerr-171][^cite_note-NMC2-172] The amended indictment did not refer to the [Cartel of the Suns](45272133.md) as an organized group as previously alleged by the US, but instead as a corrupt [patronage system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spoils_system), which aligns with experts' descriptions of its operations.[^cite_note-173]

Maduro and his wife were arraigned in a Manhattan federal court on 5 January 2026.[^cite_note-arraignmentandsomeoutcomes-174][^cite_note-175] Before pleading, Maduro said: "I am the president of Venezuela, I consider myself a [prisoner of war](25008.md). I was captured at my home in Caracas."[^cite_note-176][^cite_note-auto-40] Both pleaded not guilty to numerous drug trafficking charges. US District Judge [Alvin Hellerstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alvin_Hellerstein) ordered that Maduro be held at least until a hearing. Originally scheduled for 17 March,[^cite_note-arraignmentandsomeoutcomes-174] the hearing was delayed to 26 March.[^cite_note-177] Maduro and Flores appeared in court for it, then were transferred back to jail in Brooklyn.[^cite_note-178] Maduro is reportedly spending most of his time in prison reading the [Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible). He does not have access to newspapers or the internet in prison, but he is allowed to speak by telephone with his family and lawyers for approximately 300 minutes a month, with a maximum of 15 minutes per call.[^cite_note-179]

Their trial was scheduled for 1 June 2027.[^cite_note-180]

## Casualties

First responders in [La Guaira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Guaira) after the attack

Venezuelan defense minister [Vladimir Padrino López](45272341.md) said that US combat helicopters fired rockets and missiles in urban areas and that officials were working to gather information on the number of fatalities and injuries.[^cite_note-181] Padrino López also stated that most of Maduro's personal guard were killed during the attack.[^cite_note-newyorkerdeath-182] *[The New York Times](30680.md)* reported on 3 January 2026 that an anonymous Venezuelan official said at least 40 people, including civilians and military personnel, were killed in the attack,[^cite_note-NYT40-183] updated to 80 on 4 January, "according to a senior Venezuelan official".[^cite_note-NYT_Live_260104-35] Independent Venezuelan journalistic reports confirmed that most Venezuelan dead were among members of the presidential guard, with two civilian deaths.[^cite_note-ElDiario4Jan-184][^cite_note-185]

The presence of Cuban troops in Venezuela was made public after the attack.[^cite_note-newyorkerdeath-182] Their presence was denied as far back as 2008.[^cite_note-newyorkerdeath-182] In 2019 Maduro had claimed in an interview that no Cuban military personnel were stationed in the country and that his personal guard was Venezuelan.[^cite_note-newyorkerdeath-182] Trump indicated that Cuban forces were involved in the operation, stating: "You know, a lot of Cubans were killed yesterday."[^cite_note-186][^cite_note-EliteBodyguard-187] The government of Cuba reported that 32 Cubans were killed in Venezuela, all members of armed forces and intelligence services.[^cite_note-AJ04-12][^cite_note-188] One of the most senior Cuban soldiers that died, Colonel Humberto Roca, was responsible for [Fidel Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fidel_Castro)'s security in the past.[^cite_note-newyorkerdeath-182] According to [Reuters](18998750.md) reporting in February, anonymous Venezuelan officials indicated that some Cuban security forces and medical doctors in Venezuela were returning to Cuba, diminishing their role in Venezuelan politics. According to some officials, Cubans were being removed from their posts in Venezuela's [General Directorate of Military Counterintelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Directorate_of_Military_Counterintelligence) (DGCIM).[^cite_note-189]

On 6 January 55 military deaths were confirmed: the victims were 32 Cubans and 23 Venezuelans. Both the countries released on the same day the identities of the killed soldiers.[^cite_note-190] Also on 6 January, military forces posted a video on social media in honor of 24 soldiers killed during the attack.[^cite_note-AP24kills-31] Attorney General [Tarek William Saab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarek_William_Saab) indicated that dozens of military personnel and civilians were killed and the total number is still under investigation.[^cite_note-AP24kills-31] A day later, [Diosdado Cabello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diosdado_Cabello) said that 100 persons died during the bombing.[^cite_note-191] Independent monitors reported as of 7 January Venezuelan military casualties as of 6 January of 42[^cite_note-BBCMundo7Jan-32] or 43 soldiers (mostly at Fort Tiuna), in addition to the 32 Cuban military personnel, and 2 civilians.[^cite_note-BBCMundo7Jan-32][^cite_note-TalCual7Jan-192][^cite_note-ElPitazo6Jan-33] On 16 January, Venezuelan defense minister [Vladimir Padrino López](45272341.md), said that 47 soldiers, including 9 women, were killed during the attack.[^cite_note-reuterspadrino-34] Acting president [Delcy Rodríguez](44199228.md) declared seven days of mourning for the soldiers killed during the attack.[^cite_note-193] The remains of the Cuban fatalities were repatriated on 15 January. They received a mass funeral ceremony in [Havana](49719.md) upon arrival.[^cite_note-194]

In [Catia La Mar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catia_La_Mar), strikes hit a three-story civilian apartment complex, killing one person, seriously injuring another, and destroying the exterior wall.[^cite_note-NYTStrike-20][^cite_note-195][^cite_note-196][^cite_note-NYT40-183] [Yohana Rodríguez Sierra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_of_Yohana_Rodr%C3%ADguez), a [Colombian citizen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_nationality_law), was reported as a civilian fatality in [El Hatillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Hatillo_Municipality). The US had been bombing nearby telecommunications antennas, according to the victim's relative.[^cite_note-197][^cite_note-198]

According to the Department of Defense, seven US soldiers were injured and expected to survive. The soldiers suffered shrapnel and gunfire wounds.[^cite_note-UScasualties-14] During the [2026 State of the Union Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_State_of_the_Union_Address), Trump awarded the [Medal of Honor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medal_of_Honor) to Chief Warrant Officer [Eric Slover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Slover), one of the helicopter pilots that got injured during the operation.[^cite_note-APstateofunion-199] Slover was the lead Chinook pilot for the air assault element of the operation. His helicopter came under fire as the flight approached Maduro's compound, and he was hit in the leg and hip by four bullets. His aircraft was also hit but remained flyable. Despite his wounds, Slover maintained control of the aircraft and got the Delta Team onboard over the compound and allowed them to fast rope to the ground. He then flew back to the USS Iwo Jima located in the Caribbean Sea.[^cite_note-200]

## Further developments

### Immediate aftermath

President Trump addresses the nation on the airstrikes against Venezuela on 3 January 2026.

The government formerly led by Maduro remained in place.[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-Griffiths-2026-202] Vice President [Delcy Rodríguez](44199228.md) described Maduro as Venezuela's "only president" and called for calm and unity to defend the country amid what she called his "kidnapping". She stated that Venezuela would "never again be a colony of any empire".[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-Tiago-2026-29][^cite_note-204] On 3 January 2026, the [Supreme Tribunal of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Tribunal_of_Justice_\(Venezuela\)) ordered Rodríguez to assume the interim presidency due to Maduro's absence.[^cite_note-205] The [Venezuelan military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_military) announced it would recognize Rodríguez as acting president and called for a return to normalcy.[^cite_note-206] Rodríguez was sworn in as acting [president of Venezuela](3329692.md) on 5 January 2026 by her brother and president of the [National Assembly of Venezuela](510768.md), [Jorge Rodríguez](8767262.md).[^cite_note-Reuters2026a-43]

> But we can't take a chance after having done this incredible thing last night of letting somebody else take over where we have to do it again. We can do it again too. Nobody can stop us.

— Donald Trump on *[Fox & Friends](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_&_Friends)* on 3 January 2026, via [YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oNCn9oeQwfc\&t=1350s) [Archive.org](https://archive.org/details/FOXNEWSW_20260103_140000_Fox__Friends_Weekend/start/1380/end/1440)

The US [Federal Aviation Administration](11186.md) issued a [notice to airmen](1517785.md) on 3 January 2026 prohibiting US aircraft from operating within Venezuelan airspace, citing "ongoing military activity".[^cite_note-207][^cite_note-208] The [US Embassy in Caracas](48416680.md) issued a shelter-in-place order from its relocated operations in Colombia.[^cite_note-209] The southern area of Caracas reportedly lost power.[^cite_note-210] Republican Senator [Mike Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Lee)[^cite_note-211] said that Secretary of State [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) "anticipates no further action in Venezuela now that Maduro is in US custody,"[^cite_note-Guldogan_2026-212] and that Maduro was expected to go on trial in the US.[^cite_note-Guldogan_2026-212][^cite_note-213]

In the hours after the US attack, Rubio placed a call with Delcy and Jorge Rodríguez, as well as [Mauricio Claver-Carone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mauricio_Claver-Carone), who was involved in the plan to abduct Maduro and would go on to serve as an unofficial viceroy for the Trump administration in Venezuela.[^cite_note-\:6-51]

In a television interview with [ABC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_News_\(United_States\)), Rubio thanked news organizations who had received leaks about the operation before it took place for not reporting on it. According to *[Semafor](72046207.md)*, *The New York Times* and *The Washington Post* had received information about the attack but did not report on it in order to not endanger the lives of US personnel involved in the operation. The acknowledgment of the media withholding information about the operation came after defense secretary [Pete Hegseth](34076202.md) imposed strict restrictions on [Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) reporters due to a mistrust of journalists' abilities to withhold militarily sensitive information from the public.[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-PBSthank-135]

| External videos |
| --- |
| Maduro [perp walk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perp_walk) at New York City [Drug Enforcement Administration](146720.md) headquarters |
| video icon [X / @PaulDMauro](https://x.com/PaulDMauro/status/2007624542156660903) |

On 5 January 2026, the Venezuelan government issued an emergency order to police to arrest anyone who supported the US attack[^cite_note-215] and mobilized *[colectivos](42254804.md)* (pro-government paramilitaries) who established roadblocks and searched cars and people. Fourteen journalists were detained, but 13 of them were later released.[^cite_note-216] On 6 January, gunfire broke out in Caracas as the ministry of communication announced that police fired upon one of their own military drones "flying without permission" but that the drone suffered no damage.[^cite_note-217]

#### United States plan announcement

On 3 January 2026, Trump stated: "We are going to run the country until such time as we can do a safe, proper and judicious transition".[^cite_note-218][^cite_note-219] He indicated that the US might send troops to Venezuela as part of this.[^cite_note-Smith-2026-220] Trump also said that Rubio had made contact with Rodríguez and that she allegedly told Rubio, "We'll do whatever you need", and that she was gracious but "really doesn't have a choice";[^cite_note-221] Trump further opined that Rodríguez was "essentially willing to do what we think is necessary" to revive Venezuela,[^cite_note-NecessaryCNN-222] marking a shift in the administration's stance compared to months prior, when the US excluded any settlement with Rodríguez.[^cite_note-RodriChannel-128] Media outlets noted Rodríguez's statements contradicted Trump's.[^cite_note-Griffiths-2026-202][^cite_note-Smith-2026-220]

> "Venezuela unilaterally seized and stole American oil, American assets and American platforms, costing us billions and billions of dollars. They did this a while ago, but we never had a president that did anything about it. They took all of our property—it was our property. We built it. And we never had a president that decided to do anything about it. Instead, they fought wars that were 10,000 miles away. We built Venezuela oil industry with American talent, drive and skill. And the socialist regime stole it from us during those previous administrations, and they stole it through force. This constituted one of the largest thefts of American property in the history of our country."

— President Trump, CNN transcripts

[^cite_note-223]

Trump characterized the action as an application of the [Monroe Doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monroe_Doctrine), which he styled as the "[Donroe Doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donroe_Doctrine)",[^cite_note-224] and added that "American dominance in the western hemisphere will never be questioned again."[^cite_note-225][^cite_note-226] Trump further stated that US oil companies would "go in, spend billions of dollars, fix the badly broken infrastructure, the oil infrastructure, and start making money for the country", and that the US was prepared to launch a second, much larger attack. He also expressed openness to a US occupation of Venezuela,[^cite_note-Smith-2026-220] stating it would not cost the US anything because it would be reimbursed through revenue from Venezuela's oil reserves.[^cite_note-227] On 7 January, Trump stated that US efforts to revitalize Venezuela could take "much longer" than a year, adding that the Venezuelan authorities were doing "everything that we feel is necessary".[^cite_note-TrumpOversee-228] Regarding the reward the US government offered for information leading to Maduro's capture, Rubio added that nobody will be receiving it. Rubio said "I guess we save \$50 million" to which Trump replied "Don't let anybody claim it. Nobody deserves it but us."[^cite_note-arraignmentandsomeoutcomes-174]

On 4 January 2026, [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) retracted Trump's comments of the day before, saying the US would not govern Venezuela, but would continue to enforce the existing oil quarantine in order to pressure Venezuela for policy changes.[^cite_note-Rubio_says-229] Rubio also affirmed that the US did not officially recognize Rodríguez's presidency, despite Trump's avowal that he would work with Rodríguez.[^cite_note-RodriLegitimacy-230] According to *The New York Times*, Vice President Rodríguez's economic record of increasing her country's oil production and stabilizing its economy despite harsher US sanctions in recent years and her reputation among Venezuelan and foreign business leaders as a [technocrat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technocracy), had earned the respect of American officials.[^cite_note-Kurmanaev2026-123] For her part, Rodríguez conveyed an offer to collaborate with US officials "within the framework of international law". Of Rodríguez, Trump said that "she's cooperating", threatened her if she did not, and maintained that he would demand "total access" by US oil interests.[^cite_note-RodriMSNBC-231]

On 7 January, Marco Rubio stated that the US government has a three-stage plan for Venezuela: stability, recovery, then transition. According to Rubio, the first phase is about the stabilization of the country after the strikes and stopping it from "descending into chaos," including refining and selling up to 50 million barrels of seized Venezuelan crude oil, adding that "that money will then be handled in such a way that we will control how it is disbursed in a way that benefits the Venezuelan people, not corruption, not the regime, so we have a lot of leverage to move on the stabilization front." During the second phase of recovery, the goal is to assure foreign companies have access to Venezuelan oil trade and "also, at the same time, begin to create the process of reconciliation nationally within Venezuela, so that the opposition forces can be amnestied and released from prisons or brought back to the country, and begin to rebuild civil society." Rubio added that the final and third phase consist on overseeing a transition, "this will be a process of transition. In the end, it will be up to the Venezuelan people to transform their country."[^cite_note-232]

Trump stated there was an agreement by Venezuela to deliver a few months' worth of oil to the US, intimating that other steps, such as the freeing of political detainees and a role for the exiled opposition, might be next. However, President Rodríguez denied the existence of a deal, saying "no external agent" was dictating her decisions.[^cite_note-NYTVZoil-233]

On 8 January, the US Senate voted 52 to 47 to advance a war resolution that aimed to block the president's use of the US military to engage in military action within or against Venezuela unless authorized by Congress. The measure passed with the support of five Republican senators, but did not reach a veto-proof majority.[^cite_note-234]

#### *De facto* status as puppet state

In the months following the intervention, the United States has effectively run Venezuela as a *de facto* puppet state. Its domestic finances, government appointments, revenues, and oil reserves are overseen by the [United States Secretary of State](32293.md). The [Executive Office of the President of the United States](44029.md) has controlled the public appearances and statements by the country's leader, including *de facto* control over its foreign policy.[^cite_note-NYT_07112026-10][^cite_note-Callaghan_01042026-9] *[The Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times)* described Rodríguez as having effectively been "strong armed" by Trump "with the threat of full-scale military invasion if she does not co-operate."[^cite_note-Callaghan_01042026-9]

### Renewal of diplomatic relations

After the announcement of the release of political prisoners by the Venezuelan government on 8 January, the US and Venezuela began discussions to restart diplomatic relations, including the possibility of reopening the United States embassy in Caracas which was closed in 2019.[^cite_note-235] US officials visited Caracas and on 10 January, the US State Department published a security alert urging its citizens to leave Venezuela immediately due to the presence of *[colectivos](42254804.md)* (pro-government paramilitary groups) targeting US citizens.[^cite_note-guardianUSalert-236]

Donald Trump announced on 14 January that he had talked by phone with acting president of Venezuela Delcy Rodríguez on oil, trade and national security. The call was confirmed by Rodríguez, who qualified the phone call as positive.[^cite_note-237] Rodríguez also announced to have sent envoys to meet with United States officials the next day.[^cite_note-BloombergEnvoys14-238]

On 15 January, [Central Intelligence Agency](5183633.md) (CIA) director [John Ratcliffe](42699041.md) met with Delcy Rodríguez in Caracas to discuss cooperation and economic stability. According to a US official "The director made clear Venezuela can no longer provide support to drug traffickers like TDA" (referring to the criminal organization [Tren de Aragua](70859925.md)).[^cite_note-\:1-239]

After a discussion with Delcy Rodríguez on 29 January, Trump announced the re-opening of Venezuelan airspace.[^cite_note-240]

US diplomat [Laura Farnsworth Dogu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laura_Farnsworth_Dogu) was appointed [chargé d'affaires](856252.md) for Venezuela on 22 January,[^cite_note-AFP27Jan-241] and arrived to Venezuela on 30 January.[^cite_note-242] On 2 February, she met in [Miraflores Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miraflores_Palace) with Delcy and Jorge Rodríguez to establish "the work agenda between the Bolivarian Republic of Venezuela and the United States." After the meeting, the US embassy shared on social media that Dogu met with the Venezuelan administration to "reiterate the three phases that US Secretary of State Marco Rubio had outlined for Venezuela: stabilization, economic recovery and reconciliation, and transition." [Felix Plasencia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felix_Plasencia) was named as diplomatic representative of Venezuela to the United States.[^cite_note-\:3-50]

United States Energy Secretary [Chris Wright](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Wright) arrived to Caracas on 11 February to make an assessment on Venezuela's oil industry. He met with Delcy Rodríguez in the presidential palace. Days before, Wright said he was expecting to "visit some of the nation's oil fields to see firsthand how President Trump's historic US-Venezuela Energy Deal is unleashing peace and prosperity."[^cite_note-243] According to the Venezuelan information minister, they discussed a bilateral energy agenda. The last US Energy Secretary that visited Venezuela before that was [Bill Richardson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Richardson) in 2001 under US president [Bill Clinton](3356.md).[^cite_note-244]

Dogu and Plasencia received a US shipment of six metric tons of medicine and medical supplies that arrived in Venezuela on 13 February. Dogu indicated it was "the first of many donations" that will arrive "in the coming days". Plasencia said that it was a "message of cooperation among two sovereign countries."[^cite_note-apfirstmedicines-245]

When asked by reporters on 13 February, Trump said that he was "going to make a visit to Venezuela" but did not provide more details.[^cite_note-246] On 19 February, the chief of the [United States Southern Command](1590932.md), General [Francis L. Donovan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_L._Donovan), and senior [Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon) official Joseph Humire met with Delcy Rodríguez, Venezuelan defense minister [Vladimir Padrino López](45272341.md) and interior minister [Diosdado Cabello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diosdado_Cabello) in Caracas to discuss security.[^cite_note-247]

US Interior Secretary [Doug Burgum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doug_Burgum) visited Venezuela and met with Delcy Rodríguez on 4 March. They discussed the supply chains for critical minerals in Venezuela.[^cite_note-248] On 5 March, the US State Department and Venezuelan authorities agreed to re-establish diplomatic and consular relations.[^cite_note-249]

On 12 March, the United States declared in court that they recognized Delcy Rodríguez as the sole head of state of Venezuela.[^cite_note-250] Two days later, the flag of the United States was raised for the first time in seven years at the [Embassy of the United States, Caracas](48416680.md).[^cite_note-251] On 30 March, the United States reopened its embassy in Venezuela, meaning resumption of US diplomatic operations in Venezuela.[^cite_note-252]

On 30 April, direct commercial flights between the United States and Venezuela were resumed after a seven-year suspension.[^cite_note-253]

On 22 September, President Donald Trump met with Venezuela's interim President Delcy Rodriguez in the opening of the 81st session of the [UN General Assembly](31957.md) in New York.[^cite_note-254]

### Role of Venezuelan opposition

Influenced by a CIA analysis,[^cite_note-CIAMachado-255] Trump stated after the strikes that [María Corina Machado](5791389.md), the leader of the opposition, did not have the necessary support or respect in the country to govern.[^cite_note-Smith-2026-220][^cite_note-256] The relationships between Machado and the Trump administration as well as Venezuelan civil society had been deteriorating for months beforehand;[^cite_note-257] according to *[The Washington Post](102226.md)*, quoting two sources "close to the White House", Machado had committed the "ultimate sin" in Trump's eyes of accepting the [2025 Nobel Peace Prize](78880914.md) instead of turning it down and passing it to him.[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259] Machado confirmed on 6 January that she had no contact with Trump since October 2025 when she was announced as the Nobel winner, and voiced discontent with Trump's decision to back Rodríguez, saying she was "nothing like a moderate" and that the US expected her to take steps toward democracy.[^cite_note-260]

Machado dubbed Rodríguez "one of the main architects ... of repression", saying everyone was familiar with her record; she also insisted that she "absolutely" qualified for the leadership of the country and lauded Trump's "leadership and courage" in taking out Maduro.[^cite_note-MachPres-261]

On 15 January, María Corina Machado met privately with Donald Trump and later with multiple US senators.[^cite_note-\:2-262] Machado presented her Nobel Medal to Donald Trump as a way to sway him to give the opposition a role in determining Venezuela's future.[^cite_note-\:2-262] Machado has plans to go back to Venezuela as soon as possible. She said that "This has nothing to do with tension or relations between Delcy Rodriguez and myself," and discussed that the "criminal structure" that has dominated Venezuela for years would eventually dismantle itself.[^cite_note-\:2-262] A week later, Trump said he was considering involving Machado in his plans on Venezuela in some way, without specifying which role she would play.[^cite_note-263]

On 26 January, after a hearing on Venezuela on the US Senate, Marco Rubio met with Machado. She later said to reporters that a change in Venezuela was coming.[^cite_note-264] Venezuelan TV network [Venevisión](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venevisi%C3%B3n) broadcast Machado message after meeting with Rubio. According to the Venezuelan national press workers union, the state regulator later cut Venevision's access to some satellite TV providers.[^cite_note-wsj12feb-265] In an interview of [NBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC), Delcy Rodríguez said about Machado that "She will have to answer to Venezuela why she called upon a military intervention, why she called upon sanctions to Venezuela, and why she celebrated the actions that took place at the beginning of January."[^cite_note-wsj12feb-265]

At Machado's request, she met for a second time with Donald Trump in the White House on 6 March to review plans for her trip to come back to Venezuela.[^cite_note-266]

In September 2026, Machado's reported about seven failed attempts to enter the country by air and sea, at least three while Delcy Rodríguez was away during a UN General Assembly.[^cite_note-267]

### Political prisoner release and amnesty bill

Main article: [2026 political prisoner release in Venezuela](82304479.md)

Jorge Rodríguez announced on 8 January 2026 that an "important"[^cite_note-268][^cite_note-ElPitazoONGDenuncia-269] number of political prisoners would be released as a "gesture" by the government.[^cite_note-270] Of an estimated 800 political prisoners in Venezuela,[^cite_note-APTrickle12Jan-271][^cite_note-UN12Jan-272] nine prisoners were released that day.[^cite_note-Reuters2026b-44]

The US had previously requested the release of political prisoners, and Trump declared in an interview "they've been great ... Everything we've wanted, they've given us."[^cite_note-APprisoners-273] Trump announced on 9 January that he had cancelled a second wave of attacks due to cooperation and the release of political prisoners.[^cite_note-reuterscancelwave2-45]

The [United Nations Human Rights Council](635790.md)-mandated [Independent International Fact-Finding Mission on Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_International_Fact-Finding_Mission_on_Venezuela) reported that about 50 out of the estimated 800 political prisoners in Venezuela were released as of 12 January, saying it fell short on the Venezuelan government's international human rights obligations, and called for the "immediate and unconditional" release of all political prisoners.[^cite_note-UN12Jan-272] Local NGOs reported that those released weren't "free", as they remained subject to restrictions and trial.[^cite_note-ElPitazoONGDenuncia-269]

On 23 January, Foro Penal confirmed 154 political prisoners released since 8 January. The same day, Delcy Rodríguez announced 626 prisoners released without providing a specific date. She called for the [Office of the United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights](22463.md), led by [Volker Türk](71721726.md) to verify the lists.[^cite_note-reuters154fp-274]

On 30 January, Delcy Rodríguez announced a general amnesty bill covering the "entire period of political violence from 1999 to the present", dating back to when [Hugo Chávez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Ch%C3%A1vez) first assumed his presidency.[^cite_note-275][^cite_note-announcementamnesty-46] People convicted of murder, drug trafficking, corruption or human rights violations will not qualify under the amnesty law. The measure was long requested by the US-backed Venezuelan opposition. Opposition leader María Corina Machado said that the bill was not taken "voluntarily, but rather in response to pressure from the US government". Rodríguez also announced the shutdown of [El Helicoide](57062364.md) prison, which she said would be transformed into a cultural and sport center. Independent organizations have documented many cases of torture and human rights violation in El Helicoide. Family of some of the prisoners gathered outside El Helicoide chanting "Freedom!".[^cite_note-announcementamnesty-46]

Venezuelan opposition politician and ally of Machado, [Juan Pablo Guanipa](63556783.md), was released from prison on 8 February but taken by unknown armed groups hours later; by 10 February, his family confirmed he was under house arrest in Maracaibo, calling the measure unjust detention.[^cite_note-276]

On 19 February, the amnesty law was approved by the National Assembly of Venezuela.[^cite_note-amnestybillAPconfirmed-47] [Jorge Rodríguez](8767262.md) said that over 1557 request liberty a day after.[^cite_note-amnestybillAPconfirmed-47] Juan Pablo Guanipa was also released from house arrest.[^cite_note-277] During the [2026 State of the Union Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_State_of_the_Union_Address), Trump invited Venezuelan politician [Enrique Márquez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enrique_M%C3%A1rquez_\(Venezuelan_politician\)), who was imprisoned in 2025 and was released in early January.[^cite_note-APstateofunion-199][^cite_note-\:4-278] Trump said "Since the raid we have worked with the new leadership and they have ordered the closure of that vile prison and released hundreds of political prisoners already, with more to come."[^cite_note-\:4-278]

### Oil deals, privatization reform, mining and ease of sanctions

Secretary of State Rubio testifies on Trump's military actions in Venezuela before the [Senate Foreign Relations Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_Foreign_Relations_Committee) on 28 January 2026.

On 14 January, the [United States Department of Energy](62866.md) announced that the United States had completed their first sales of Venezuelan oil valued at \$500 million as part of a \$2 billion deal between United States and Venezuela governments.[^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280] On 15 January, Delcy Rodríguez announced that she was submitting a reform to the country's [hydrocarbon law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_involvement_in_Venezuela's_petroleum_industry#Hydrocarbon_Law_of_1943) to "allow \[foreign] investment flows to be incorporated into new fields, fields where no investment has ever been made and into fields where there is no infrastructure";[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282] On 20 January, Delcy Rodríguez confirmed having received the first \$300 million. She announced the money will go to the exchange market in Venezuela, the national banks and the [central bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Bank_of_Venezuela).[^cite_note-first300M-53] [Reuters](18998750.md) indicated that the remaining \$200 million were returned on 3 February according to anonymous United States officials.[^cite_note-283] The proceeds from the sales were deposited in a Qatari bank account, preventing Venezuela's creditors from seizing them. Democrats questioned the legality of this arrangement, and criticized the lack of transparency in routing the funds.[^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285]

On 23 January, the first cargo of 460,000 barrels of [naphtha](184342.md) by [Vitol](17518274.md), necessary to deal with the [heavy crude oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_crude_oil), arrived to Venezuela in a deal with the United States. Before that last cargo was received in December 2025 by [Chevron Corporation](284749.md) in an agreement with the United States as the naval blockade had stopped many suppliers.[^cite_note-286]

On 28 January, Delcy Rodríguez announced that the United States was unfreezing various funds related to 2019 oil sanctions on Venezuela.[^cite_note-AFP27Jan-241] The next day, Reuters announced that an US refiner, [Citgo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citgo), bought Venezuelan oil for the first time since 2019.[^cite_note-287]

On the US Senate hearing on 28 January, US secretary of state Marco Rubio stated that "The funds from that (oil sales) will be deposited into an account that we will have oversight over," Rubio said, adding that the US Treasury would audit the expenses of the Venezulan government only on sanctioned oil so that it is used in favor of medicines or measures that would help the Venezuelan population. He said, "will spend that money for the benefit of the Venezuelan people."[^cite_note-288]

Rodríguez signed the hydrocarbon reform into law on 29 January.[^cite_note-signlaw-54][^cite_note-reuterssignlaw-289] The law allows private and foreign companies to operate oil projects under contracts over production and sales, lowers certain taxes, expands the oil ministry's authority, and permits asset transfers and outsourcing. Proposals by opposition lawmakers on grant transparency and require National Assembly approval for oil contracts were rejected.[^cite_note-reuterssignlaw-289] Oil industry workers participated in a demonstration to celebrate the bill approval. The law reverted 2006 oil industry changes by [Hugo Chávez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Ch%C3%A1vez) to make state company [PDVSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PDVSA) the main stakeholder in all oil projects.[^cite_note-signlaw-54] In parallel, the US Treasury's Office of Foreign Assets Control lifted various oil-related sanctions imposed on Venezuela, authorizing US companies to buy, sell, transport, store and refine Venezuelan crude oil. US sanctions on production of oil were not lifted. Trump administration also announced that additional sanctions will be lifted soon.[^cite_note-firsteasesanctions-55]

More sanctions were eased on 13 February, with the issue of two licenses allowing international companies to operate oil and gas projects in Venezuela and to negotiate contracts. Payments go to a US-controlled foreign government deposit fund. US energy secretary that visited the Venezuela in February, said that oil sales have hit \$1 billion since January and would hit another \$5 billion in months.[^cite_note-reuterssanctions2licences-56]

The US Treasury Department indicated that companies working of Venezuelan oil trade have to pay local taxes, permits and fees to the government of Venezuela, while royalties and federal taxes must be paid into a fund managed by the United States.[^cite_note-290]

During the [2026 State of the Union Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_State_of_the_Union_Address), Trump announced that the US "just received from our new friend and partner, Venezuela, more than 80 million barrels of oil."[^cite_note-APstateofunion-199]

According to the Reuters in February, the Venezuelan oil ministry cancelled 19 contracts with private companies on oil and gas in Venezuela signed under the government of Nicolás Maduro. Venezuelan and US administrations are reviewing the credentials of the companies that signed them.[^cite_note-291]

In March, the United States issued a license to authorize the trade of Venezuelan gold and allow deals with Minarven, Venezuela's state-owned gold mining company.[^cite_note-292]

On 28 August 2026, President Donald Trump announced that the United States had reached an oil agreement with Venezuela's interim government, led by [Delcy Rodríguez](44199228.md). Trump stated on his social media platform that the agreement would give the United States majority control over 65 billion barrels of proven oil reserves in Venezuela. He said the deal had been brokered through a partnership with private business and mediated by Secretary of State [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) and Secretary of Defense [Pete Hegseth](34076202.md). Trump claimed that the agreement came "at no cost to the American taxpayer" and would more than double U.S. oil reserves. Few details were disclosed regarding the legal and financial structure of the agreement, including which fields or companies were involved, or how the United States would exercise majority control over the reserves.[^cite_note-293][^cite_note-294]

*[The Washington Post](102226.md)* reported that under the arrangement the United States had gained a controlling stake in nearly a quarter of Venezuela's untapped oil reserves, becoming the majority owner of a newly formed joint venture holding 100-year contracts at the fields, and that a list of 17 oil fields in which the United States would take a stake had circulated before the announcement. According to deal points reviewed by the newspaper, the United States government's stake would be split between equity ownership and guarantees that it would purchase oil produced by the venture at cost to refill the [Strategic Petroleum Reserve](167198.md). Several of the fields lacked infrastructure or access to transport hubs, and those where such infrastructure existed had suffered years of neglect and theft of machinery; the newspaper reported that it was unclear who would make the tens of billions of dollars of investment needed before oil could be pumped, major oil companies having largely regarded such spending as too risky. Secretary of State [Marco Rubio](5502549.md) said the agreement would bring nearly \$100 billion in private investment to Venezuela, without specifying which companies had committed to such spending.[^cite_note-WaPo-2026-08-28-295]

The principal private Venezuelan participant would be North American Blue Energy Partners, controlled by [Alejandro Betancourt López](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alejandro_Betancourt_L%C3%B3pez), a businessman who has been the subject of money-laundering investigations in several countries and who has not been charged. The [Defense Department](7279897.md), through its Office of Strategic Capital, would oversee and help fund the oil field licenses, according to people familiar with the discussions; [Bloomberg News](3057255.md) first reported the involvement of the company and the Pentagon. Pentagon spokesman Sean Parnell said the office could not take an ownership stake in private companies and that its role was "strictly limited to providing capital assistance in the form of a loan, loan guarantee, or technical assistance". The newspaper noted that any United States expenditures or loans needed to develop the fields would probably require congressional approval, and that earlier announcements of United States stakes in Ukraine's critical minerals and in [Westinghouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westinghouse_Electric_Company) had not produced the developments the administration had forecast.[^cite_note-WaPo-2026-08-28-295] *[The New York Times](30680.md)* reported that the [State Department](31975.md) put the United States' share at 55 per cent of the joint venture's output, and that the United States would hold the right to acquire shares in the Venezuelan partner through a [warrant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warrant_\(finance\)).[^cite_note-NYT-2026-08-29-296] [Delcy Rodríguez](44199228.md) endorsed the agreement, saying it would have a "significant impact on our nation's revival" and generate more than \$200 billion in tax revenue for the government.[^cite_note-NYT-2026-08-29-296] The newspaper reported that [Chevron](284749.md) was separately in advanced talks to expand its own production in the country.[^cite_note-NYT-2026-08-29-296] It also reported that any meaningful new production would take years to materialise, so the agreement would have little immediate effect on global commodity prices or on what United States consumers pay for fuel; Venezuela was producing a little over one million barrels a day, about one per cent of global output and roughly the same share as under Maduro.[^cite_note-NYT-2026-08-29-296]

The announcement followed earlier reports that the Trump administration had been in advanced negotiations to acquire a direct stake in Venezuelan oilfields. These reports had already generated unease among members of Venezuela's opposition. One opposition figure, speaking anonymously due to the sensitivities of criticizing the United States, described the proposed arrangement as "a land grab – a massive land grab," adding that it was "revolting" and represented "a rapacious, mafioso United States," in contrast to the United States of the [Marshall Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marshall_Plan).[^cite_note-f-297]

Ricardo Hausmann, a former Venezuelan minister in exile and opposition supporter, wrote on [X](9988187.md) that an illegitimate interim government lacked the legitimacy to sign what he termed an unconstitutional agreement, and predicted that it would fail for all parties involved.
