[Francisco Rodríguez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_Rodr%C3%ADguez_\(economist\)), an economist, called on [Venezuela's National Assembly](510768.md) to reject the deal, describing it as "predatory" and arguing that transferring Venezuelan oil wealth to the United States was unconstitutional and not in the country's interest, particularly when carried out under the threat of force. Gregory Brew, an energy historian and analyst at the Eurasia Group, expressed skepticism about the agreement, predicting significant opposition to any deal that would give the U.S. government effective control over Venezuelan resources. He compared the arrangement to the early 20th-century control exercised by the Anglo-Persian Oil Company over oil resources in Iraq and Iran, and suggested that for Trump, control over oil appeared to be an objective in itself, with the arrangement carrying colonial overtones.[^cite_note-f-297]

On 31 August, *The New York Times* reported that the arrangement would give the Defense Department the option to acquire up to 35 per cent of the parent company of Betancourt's firm through a warrant, described by two people familiar with the deal as a penny warrant convertible into shares for as little as one cent. The White House said the same day that the partnership came "all at zero cost to the United States", that the federal government would be guaranteed 20 per cent of the company's output at production cost with the State Department holding a right of first refusal over the remainder, and that the United States would hold a veto over appointments to the company's board, a majority of whose members would have to be American citizens. The statement contradicted Parnell's assertion that the Office of Strategic Capital does not take equity stakes in private companies. The office, established in 2022 under defense secretary [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin) to lend to firms supplying the defense industrial base, reports to deputy defense secretary [Stephen A. Feinberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Feinberg), who approves its transactions, and its lending authority had grown from \$1 billion to \$100 billion. [Jack Reed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Reed_\(Rhode_Island_politician\)), the ranking Democrat on the [Senate Armed Services Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Senate_Committee_on_Armed_Services), called the effort "a blatant abuse of power and taxpayer dollars" and demanded a full accounting of its legal authority and financial terms.[^cite_note-NYT-2026-08-31-298]

### Elections in Venezuela

Main article: [Next Venezuelan presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Next_Venezuelan_presidential_election)

According to the [Constitution of Venezuela](1211684.md), in the event of the president becoming permanently unavailable to serve, a new election must be held within 30 days; in the event of them becoming temporarily unable to serve, the vice president acts as president for up to 90 days, which can be extended by vote of the National Assembly. The Supreme Court ruled that his absence was temporary, not permanent.[^cite_note-299]

[Nicolás Maduro Guerra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro_Guerra), Venezuelan congressman and son of Nicolás Maduro, declared on 27 January 2026 that elections in Venezuela are "not on the table" as his father was "kidnapped".[^cite_note-AFP27Jan-241]

On the US Senate hearing on 28 January, US secretary of state Marco Rubio was questioned about Venezuela. When discussing the goal of the intervention he answered "What's the end state? We want a Venezuela that has legitimate democratic elections."[^cite_note-300]

In an interview for *[Politico](9172097.md)* in February, Machado said that she had not spoken with Trump about elections in Venezuela, but she estimated that with manual voting an election could be held in 9 to 10 months.[^cite_note-301] In an interview for [Newsmax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newsmax), Jorge Rodríguez ruled out elections in the short term, stating "The only thing I can say is that there will be no elections in this immediate period in which stabilization must be achieved."[^cite_note-302]

On 24 July, U.S. President Trump said that Venezuela was not ready to hold elections, but that it was making a lot of progress under acting President Rodriguez who has "been doing a fantastic job".[^cite_note-reuters-20260724-303]

### Joint arrest operation and extradition of Saab

Maduro's industry minister [Alex Saab](61390840.md), released from the United States in 2023 in a prisoners exchange with Venezuela, was dismissed from his role on 16 January by acting president Delcy Rodríguez. She merged the industry and the commerce ministry.[^cite_note-304] According to US officials, Saab and the head of the [Globovisión](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globovisi%C3%B3n) TV network [Raúl Gorrín](61258343.md) were arrested in February by [Bolivarian National Intelligence Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolivarian_National_Intelligence_Service) (SEBIN) in a joint operation between Venezuela and the United States. They are expected to be extradited to the United States.[^cite_note-305]

The Venezuelan government announced on 16 May that Saab was deported to the United States. He is a possible witness against the prosecution of Nicolás Maduro.[^cite_note-\:5-306]

### Alleged insider betting prior to the operation



Indictment of Gannon Ken Van Dyke

On 23 April 2026, U.S. Army Master Sergeant Gannon Ken Van Dyke was arrested for allegedly placing more than US\$33,300 in bets on the prediction site [Polymarket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polymarket) based around whether or not Maduro would be "out by January 31, 2026" ("out" likely meaning removed from office). When the bets were successful, Van Dyke gained more than US\$409,000 in winnings. According to an unsealed indictment in the Southern District of New York, Van Dyke was charged with theft of nonpublic government information, commodities fraud, wire fraud, unlawful use of confidential government information for personal gain, and engaging in monetary transactions from unlawful activity. According to the Justice Department, Van Dyke was involved in the planning and execution of the operation to capture Maduro.[^cite_note-307][^cite_note-308] Van Dyke entered a not guilty plea on 28 April 2026.[^cite_note-309] A federal judge set the tentative date of Van Dyke's trial to 7 December 2026.[^cite_note-310]

### Joint operation against Niño Guerrero

In June 2026, a military operation was carried to kill Héctor Rusthenford Guerrero Flores, known as [Niño Guerrero](80966886.md), the leader of the Venezuelan criminal gang [Tren de Aragua](70859925.md). US president Donald Trump announced that Guerrero was killed in a airstrike on 12 June in Venezuela conducted in coordination with Venezuelan authorities. Venezuelan officials confirmed their participation in the operation.[^cite_note-guerrero-311]

## Reactions and response

### Venezuela







**Clockwise from the top**:&#x20;

- Vice President Rodríguez leads a meeting at [Miraflores Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miraflores_Palace) following the US strikes in Venezuela, on 3 January 2026

- Rodríguez leads the 757th Council of Ministers meeting on 4 January 2026

- The [Supreme Tribunal of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Tribunal_of_Justice_\(Venezuela\)) order Rodríguez to become the acting head of state

Before Maduro's capture, the [Venezuelan government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_government) said it rejected and denounced the military aggression,[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-DiazWalsh030126-157][^cite_note-BBC-131][^cite_note-313] and issued a public statement blaming the [US government](195149.md) for the strikes, which it attributed to a plan to seize Venezuelan resources, particularly oil reserves.[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315] Defense Minister [Vladimir Padrino López](45272341.md) stated that the attacks were illegal.[^cite_note-NBC_Live-125] He also said that Venezuela would resist foreign troops[^cite_note-316] and that the government had declared a "state of external commotion", a type of [state of emergency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States_of_emergency_in_Venezuela), following "Maduro's orders".[^cite_note-317]

Vice President Delcy Rodríguez stated that Venezuela "will never again be anyone's colony – neither of old empires, nor of new empires, nor of empires in decline". She argued that the "[war on drugs](1181646.md)" was a pretext, claiming the United States' true objective was regime change and control of the country's "energy, mineral and natural resources".[^cite_note-Smith-2026-220] Rodríguez also referred to the US attack on Venezuela as "[Zionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionist)".[^cite_note-Zionist-318]

[Unitary Platform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_Platform) politician [María Corina Machado](5791389.md) published a statement from exile, saying that "what had to happen" for the opposition to take up the mandate they claimed from the 2024 presidential election "is happening".[^cite_note-319] She also said the presidency should be assumed by [Edmundo González](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmundo_Gonz%C3%A1lez), who received a majority of the vote in the 2024 presidential election, according to international observers and election-watchers.[^cite_note-APReview-320][^cite_note-ElEspectador_decimal_places-321][^cite_note-AtlanticDictatorLies-322] González said the opposition was ready to "rebuild our nation" and that the next few hours would be "decisive".[^cite_note-323] Machado, who won the 2025 Nobel Peace Prize, offered to share her award with Trump, calling the United States strike as "historic" and thanking him on behalf of the Venezuelan people. The Nobel Prize Committee rejected her request and clarified that the prize "cannot be revoked, shared or transferred".[^cite_note-324]

### United States

According to Trump, the US would "run" Venezuela until there is a "safe, proper and judicious transition". Trump went on to say that the US and US companies would seize Venezuelan oil and sell it to other countries.[^cite_note-Run-325] Trump said the US would be "very strongly involved" in the [Venezuelan oil industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_oil_industry),[^cite_note-McKelvie_Guardian-39] and that US oil companies would invest billions of dollars in the energy sector of the country.[^cite_note-326] Secretary of Defense [Pete Hegseth](34076202.md) said "We are going to get American companies in there" to invest in the Venezuelan oil industry, resulting in "access to additional wealth and resources" for the US.[^cite_note-327][^cite_note-Run-325][^cite_note-Cawthorne2026-102] The government also indicated that American oil companies would have to invest in rebuilding Venezuelan oil infrastructure as a prerequisite for being compensated for previous [asset seizures by the Venezuelan government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PDVSA#List_of_Nationalized_Companies) since the first nationalization nearly 50 years ago.[^cite_note-politicoprivateoil-328]

Vice President [JD Vance](53396477.md) stated that the operation was justified as Maduro was wanted in the US. He also declared that "the stolen oil must be returned".[^cite_note-329] Chairwoman [Lisa McClain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lisa_McClain) of the [House Republican Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Republican_Conference) called Maduro a "narcoterrorist" and said that "Trump didn't look the other way; he acted."[^cite_note-House_GOP_Chair-330] After initially expressing uncertainty about the justification of US actions, Republican senator [Mike Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Lee) later said they "likely" fell under the president's [Article II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_Two_of_the_United_States_Constitution) powers to protect against "actual or imminent attack".[^cite_note-CNN-2026-152][^cite_note-Cano_2026-136]

Florida Senators [Rick Scott](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Scott) and [Ashley Moody](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashley_Moody), along with Representative [Carlos Gimenez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_A._Gim%C3%A9nez), held a press conference in [Doral](109422.md) praising the capture of Maduro. Scott, who said he received a phone call from [Secretary of State Marco Rubio](5502549.md) early Saturday morning, also praised Venezuelan opposition leader [María Corina Machado](5791389.md) for her role in the events. Gimenez stated that [Trump](4848272.md) had also "turned his eye" toward Cuba, noting that "the Cuban regime is at the weakest point it's been in a very, very long time."[^cite_note-331] Governor [Ron DeSantis](36729527.md) supported the operation and said that the [Attorney General of Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney_General_of_Florida) was “looking very seriously” at the possibility of filing state charges against Maduro aside from the federal indictment, accusing him of bring drugs and violent criminals to [Florida](18933066.md).[^cite_note-332]

Democratic senator [Brian Schatz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Schatz), a member of the [Senate Foreign Relations Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Senate_Committee_on_Foreign_Relations), warned against any US military involvement in Venezuela, saying that the United States has "no vital national interests in Venezuela to justify war".[^cite_note-CNN-2026-152] Democratic senator [Ruben Gallego](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruben_Gallego) said "this war is illegal".[^cite_note-Gallego-2026-333][^cite_note-CBS_News-2026-38] Democratic senator [Tim Kaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Kaine) condemned the actions and called on Congress to support his resolution to block the use of the armed forces against Venezuela unless authorized by Congress.[^cite_note-ABC_News:_Republicans_largely_back_Trump-133] Senator [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders) also condemned the strikes, citing a risk of global instability and accusing Trump of bypassing Congress to get the country into war.[^cite_note-334][^cite_note-335] Former vice president [Kamala Harris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamala_Harris) criticized the strikes.[^cite_note-336] The mayor of New York City, [Zohran Mamdani](64618347.md), said he called Trump earlier that day to express his opposition to the strikes.[^cite_note-Run-325]

In a 3 January 2026 editorial, *[The New York Times](30680.md)* editorial board condemned the attack as "dangerous and illegal", describing it as an act of "latter-day imperialism" which lacked "any semblance of international legitimacy, valid legal authority or domestic endorsement". Comparing the attack to [interventions in Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_military_intervention_in_Libya), the [war in Afghanistan](19666611.md), and [US involvement in Nicaragua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contras#U.S._military_and_financial_assistance), it warned of the "potential for chaos in Venezuela", including violence from paramilitary groups such as the Venezuelan [colectivos](42254804.md) or the Colombian [National Liberation Army](822032.md) (ELN), which operates in western Venezuela.[^cite_note-337] The [AFL-CIO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFL-CIO) condemned the attack, calling it unconstitutional.[^cite_note-\:0-338] Many analysts liken the operation in Venezuela to prior operations, such as [Operation Nifty Package](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Nifty_Package) which led to the capture of Panamanian military leader, [Manuel Noriega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Noriega),[^cite_note-339] as well as [Operation Red Dawn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Red_Dawn), which led to the capture of former Iraqi president, [Saddam Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saddam_Hussein).[^cite_note-340]

### Cuba

See also: [2026 Cuban crisis](82348646.md)

In January 2026, [First Secretary of the Communist Party of Cuba](38776884.md) [Miguel Díaz-Canel](38630938.md) strongly condemned strikes in Venezuela and the subsequent capture of President Nicolás Maduro, labeling the actions as "[state terrorism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_terrorism)".[^cite_note-341] On 15 January 2026, he was present, alongside 94-year-old former Cuban leader [Raúl Castro](221749.md), in a ceremony in [Havana](49719.md) to pay tribute to 32 Cuban soldiers killed in the United States intervention in Venezuela.[^cite_note-342]

### Threats to other countries

Trump set his sights on a number of countries in the following days.[^cite_note-343] Trump signaled an eagerness to apply pressure on Colombia and said when asked that a similar operation there "sounds good to me". Trump threatened Colombia's president [Gustavo Petro](13391962.md), saying he has "cocaine mills and cocaine factories" and "likes making cocaine and selling it to the United States" but would not be doing it much longer. Trump had recently sanctioned the Colombian leader for his alleged reluctance to combat [drug trafficking in the country](13005346.md). Petro promised to take up arms if he had to, and Colombia's [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Foreign_Affairs_\(Colombia\)) issued a statement opposing "an undue interference" in domestic politics.[^cite_note-TrumpColo-344][^cite_note-ColomThreat-345][^cite_note-Guardian-Brodzinsky-346] Days later Trump and Petro agreed in a phone call to work together on combating drug trafficking and domestic insurgency, with Petro demanding that Trump "strike hard" against the [ELN](822032.md) in both Venezuela and Colombia; Petro said fighting the ELN "in the rear" was just as critical as attacking it inside Colombia.[^cite_note-ELNTrump-347]

Trump threatened Mexico and Cuba, saying the two nations needed to do more to earn US favor, but said the latter might "fall" without US interference; he also expressed hope that Mexico would act on its own.[^cite_note-HuffCuba-348] In an appearance on *[Hannity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Sean_Hannity_Show)* Trump said that "We are going to start now hitting land with regard to the cartels. The cartels are running Mexico".[^cite_note-CartelsRun-349] *[Newsweek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newsweek)* noted that Trump had said land strikes were imminent before, but also that he had been doing the groundwork by designating cartels [Foreign Terrorist Organizations](421835.md).[^cite_note-350] Trump also warned that [Iran](14653.md) "would be hit very hard" if more demonstrators were killed during the [protests occurring in the country](81976180.md).[^cite_note-BBC-Bennett-351]

#### Greenland

See also: [Greenland crisis](82044223.md)

Trump renewed his talk of annexing [Greenland](12118.md), an [autonomous territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_territory) of the [Kingdom of Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Denmark), with [CNBC](223441.md) noting that this prompted more serious concerns in the wake of Maduro's capture.[^cite_note-CNBC-Breuninger6Jan-352] The US already operates a military base in Greenland,[^cite_note-353] [Pituffik Space Base](750735.md), and Denmark has indicated its openness to a heightened US military presence there, but Trump has been uninterested in anything less than a full US takeover,[^cite_note-354] noting "that's what I feel is psychologically needed for success. I think that ownership gives you a thing that you can't do with, you're talking about a lease or a treaty. Ownership gives you things and elements that you can't get from just signing a document"[^cite_note-NYT-Sanger-355] and "When we own it, we defend it. You don't defend leases the same way, you have to own it".[^cite_note-CNBC-Breuninger9Jan-356] Asked "Psychologically important to you or to the United States?", he said: "Psychologically important for me."[^cite_note-357]

Hours after the strikes [Katie Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katie_Miller), wife of White House Deputy Chief of Staff [Stephen Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Miller), posted a picture of a Greenland in the colors of the American flag with the caption "SOON" on [X](9988187.md), prompting a rebuke from Denmark's ambassador to the US that "we expect full respect" for Denmark's [territorial integrity](31335.md) and the two countries, being close allies, should continue to work as such.[^cite_note-358] Over the following days, Stephen Miller asked "by what right does Denmark assert control over Greenland?",[^cite_note-ABC-Khan-359] said Denmark and nations in general unable to defend their territory forgo their claim,[^cite_note-360] and that it was both the formal position of the US government[^cite_note-BBC-Sheerin-361] and obvious for security that Greenland should be part of the US.[^cite_note-ABC-Khan-359] Asked repeatedly whether he would rule out taking Greenland by force,[^cite_note-NYT-Cameron-362] he refused but said that such questions are moot: "Nobody's going to fight the United States militarily over the future of Greenland."[^cite_note-CNBC-Breuninger6Jan-352]

Trump emphasized the acquisition of Greenland by the US "from the standpoint of national security".[^cite_note-BBC-Bennett-351] The leaders of [NATO](21133.md) members [France](5843419.md), [Germany](11867.md), [Italy](14532.md), [Poland](22936.md), [Spain](26667.md), and the [United Kingdom](31717.md) expressed their support for Denmark in a joint statement, stating that "Greenland belongs to its people, and only Denmark and Greenland can decide on matters concerning their relations", stressed they were keen on Arctic security but this had to be achieved by NATO allies collectively, and called for upholding the principles of the [UN Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Charter), including [sovereignty](28791.md), territorial integrity, and the inviolability of borders.[^cite_note-BBC-Sheerin-361] Hours later,[^cite_note-BBC-Sheerin-361] White House press secretary [Karoline Leavitt](71739774.md) said that acquiring Greenland was "a national security priority of the United States", "vital to deter our adversaries in the Arctic region", and added: "The president and his team are discussing a range of options to pursue this important foreign policy goal, and of course, utilizing the US military is always an option at the commander in chief's disposal."[^cite_note-363] Rubio emphasized that any acquisition of Greenland by the US would be negotiated rather than resort to military force, saying Trump's threats were mainly rhetorical and an effort to push Denmark into approving a US-friendly deal.[^cite_note-WSJRubioGreenland-364] In the following days Trump said the US is "going to do something \[there] whether they like it or not",[^cite_note-365] that "We're not going to have Russia or China occupy Greenland. That's what they're going to do if we don't. So we're going to be doing something with Greenland, either the nice way or the more difficult way"[^cite_note-366] and "I would like to make a deal, you know, the easy way. But if we don't do it the easy way, we're going to do it the hard way," that "the fact that they had a boat land there 500 years ago doesn't mean that they own the land. \[I'm] sure we had lots of boats go there also."[^cite_note-CNBC-Breuninger9Jan-356] Asked if obtaining Greenland or preserving NATO was his higher priority, he said "it may be a choice."[^cite_note-NYT-Sanger-355]

### International

Main article: [International reactions to the 2026 United States intervention in Venezuela](82023556.md)



Reactions to the intervention:

&#x20; Countries that supported the US operation

&#x20; Countries that called for a democratic transition in Venezuela

&#x20; Countries that called for respect for international law

&#x20; Countries that condemned the intervention

Following the strikes, Venezuela's neighbors acted to contain fallout. [Colombian](5222.md) president [Gustavo Petro](13391962.md) ordered the mobilization of security forces along the [Colombia–Venezuela border](47414233.md) in anticipation of a mass influx of Venezuelan refugees.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368] On 5 January 2026, Petro, who was a former guerrilla fighter, warned that he would "take up arms" for his country if necessary after the US threatened him and his government, stating that any similar interventions in Colombia would have a response.[^cite_note-369] On 8 January, thousands of Colombians protested against the intervention and the capture of Maduro, including the city of [Cúcuta](623811.md), near the Venezuelan border.[^cite_note-370] On 10 January, the head of the largest [FARC dissidents](57074954.md) group, [Nestor Gregorio Vera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nestor_Gregorio_Vera?action=edit\&redlink=1), called for other guerrilla groups to unite to face any US intervention.[^cite_note-371]

Police in [Trinidad and Tobago](3565457.md), which hosts the largest per-capita population of Venezuelan refugees and migrants,[^cite_note-Herbert-2021-372] cordoned off key areas of the capital, [Port of Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Spain).[^cite_note-373] The [government of Trinidad and Tobago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago), which has hosted US military personnel and vessels amid its recent [buildup in the Caribbean Sea](80987786.md), stated that it did not participate in the attack.[^cite_note-CNN-2026-152] The government of [Guyana](17238691.md), whose [ongoing border dispute with Venezuela heated up in 2023–2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guyana%E2%80%93Venezuela_crisis_\(2023%E2%80%932024\)), had a muted response but were broadly supportive of the removal of Maduro. Guyanese president [Irfaan Ali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irfaan_Ali) announced that the country's security forces were "fully mobilised" to deal with potential refugees and an increase in instability along the border.[^cite_note-374] The [Cuban](5042481.md) government declared two days of national mourning on 5 and 6 January 2026 for its soldiers killed in the attacks.[^cite_note-375] Later on 16 January, tens of thousands of Cubans gathered in the [José Martí Anti-Imperialist Platform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mart%C3%AD_Anti-Imperialist_Platform) outside the [US embassy in Havana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embassy_of_the_United_States,_Havana) to demonstrate against US actions in Venezuela, the killings of 32 Cuban officers in the attack, and to demand the release of Maduro.[^cite_note-376] On 10 January, [Nicaraguan](21362.md) authorities arrested over 60 people for supporting the capture of Maduro.[^cite_note-377]

UN Secretary-General [António Guterres](471806.md) stated that US actions had "worrying implications for the region", "constitute\[d] a dangerous precedent", and worried "that the rules of international law have not been respected".[^cite_note-United_Nations_News-2026-378] He called on all Venezuelan actors to engage in an inclusive and democratic dialogue, stating "I welcome and am ready to support all efforts aimed at assisting Venezuelans in finding a peaceful way forward".[^cite_note-379] At the request of China, Colombia, and Russia, the [UNSC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNSC) convened on 5 January 2026 to discuss the situation.[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381] Representatives of Russia and China called for the immediate release of President Maduro while the US representative rejected characterizations of US actions as military aggression, describing the operation as a targeted law enforcement measure to arrest an indicted fugitive.[^cite_note-382]

The attack was condemned by other countries, including [Brazil](3383.md),[^cite_note-Euronews-2026-01-04-383] [Chile](5489.md),[^cite_note-384] [China](5405.md),[^cite_note-385] [France](5843419.md),[^cite_note-386] [Iran](14653.md),[^cite_note-387] [Mexico](3966054.md),[^cite_note-388] [North Korea](21255.md),[^cite_note-389] [Russia](25391.md),[^cite_note-390] [Slovakia](26830.md),[^cite_note-391] [South Africa](17416221.md),[^cite_note-392] and [Spain](26667.md).[^cite_note-Euronews-2026-01-04-383] Government leaders in [Argentina](18951905.md), [France](5843419.md),[^cite_note-2026-01-03-wsj-393] [Germany](11867.md),[^cite_note-2026-01-03-wsj-393] [Israel](9282173.md),[^cite_note-394] [Peru](170691.md),[^cite_note-395] and [Ukraine](31750.md) praised the developments.[^cite_note-396] French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) initially expressed joy, declaring on social media that "Maduro is a dictator, and his departure is good news for Venezuelans. He confiscated the freedom of his people and stole the [2024 elections](60825396.md)." However, days later he nuanced his message, saying that the method employed by the Americans "does not have the support or approval" of France.[^cite_note-397] [United Kingdom](31717.md) prime minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md) said that he was happy for the fall of Maduro, and he would wait to know the facts before commenting further.[^cite_note-398] [José Antonio Kast](55052190.md), the president-elect of Chile, stated that Maduro was not the legitimate president of Venezuela, had "destabilized Latin America", called his capture "great news", and remarked that work remained and the "governments of Latin America must ensure that the entire apparatus of the regime relinquishes power and is held accountable".[^cite_note-399] Spanish prime minister [Pedro Sánchez](43297512.md) urged de-escalation, stating that "\[i]nternational law and the principles of the U.N. Charter must be respected"; this was echoed by [European Union](9317.md) (EU) foreign policy chief and [European Commission](9974.md) vice-president [Kaja Kallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaja_Kallas).[^cite_note-400][^cite_note-CNN-2026-152] The governments of Brazil, Colombia, Mexico, Spain, Uruguay, and the outgoing administration in Chile issued a joint statement rejecting the US action and expressed their concern saying that it could "constitute an extremely dangerous precedent for peace and regional security and endanger the civilian population".[^cite_note-401]

Several international trade union organizations, including the [International Trade Union Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Trade_Union_Confederation), [Trade Union Confederation of the Americas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trade_Union_Confederation_of_the_Americas), and the [World Federation of Trade Unions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Federation_of_Trade_Unions), condemned the attack.[^cite_note-\:0-338]

Environmental NGOs, including [Greenpeace](12233.md) and Oil Change International, have criticised the United States' oil-motivated actions in Venezuela and reiterated calls for a [just transition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Just_transition) away from fossil fuels.[^cite_note-402]

The [Federal Council](218299.md) of [Switzerland](26748.md) decided to freeze any assets held in the country by Nicolás Maduro.[^cite_note-403]

### Celebrations





Venezuelans in [Santiago](51572.md), Chile, celebrating Maduro's capture (top), demonstrators outside of Nicolás Maduro's arraignment in [New York City](645042.md) gathering in support of Maduro's capture (bottom)

Following Maduro's capture, demonstrations celebrating his removal and opposing his presidency occurred in Caracas, the capital and largest city of Venezuela.[^cite_note-404][^cite_note-Macaya-2026-41]

[Venezuelans living abroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_diaspora) celebrated Maduro's removal across the United States, Spain, and Latin America. In [Doral, Florida](109422.md), where the largest population of Venezuelans in the US live, Venezuelans filled the streets waving flags, singing, and chanting "freedom".[^cite_note-405] Thousands of Venezuelans celebrated Maduro's fall in [Madrid](41188263.md), Spain.[^cite_note-406] Videos from the Colombian border city of [Cúcuta](623811.md) showed crowds filling the streets on the night of 3 January 2026, dancing to music and chanting slogans like "virtue" and "a free Venezuela".[^cite_note-Macaya-2026-41] Similar celebrations by Venezuelan expatriates and refugees took place in [Buenos Aires](255919.md) in [Argentina](18951905.md), [Lima](85423.md) in [Peru](170691.md), [Quito](209982.md) in [Ecuador](9334.md), and [Santiago](51572.md) in [Chile](5489.md).[^cite_note-Reuters-20252-407][^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409]

### Protests





Demonstration in [Times Square](31389.md) against the attack (left), demonstrators in [Catia La Mar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catia_La_Mar), Venezuela, protesting against US involvement (right)

Maduro's supporters protested against his capture in Caracas, with protesters holding portraits of Maduro and pro-Maduro and pro-*[Partido Socialista Unido de Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partido_Socialista_Unido_de_Venezuela)* (PSUV) banners. PSUV called for citizens to demand the release of Maduro and Flores.[^cite_note-410] CNN reported that while some Venezuelans celebrated Maduro's ousting, others were skeptical of Trump's motives and condemned the capture of Maduro as a "kidnapping".[^cite_note-Macaya-2026-41]

In the United States, hundreds of people gathered in New York City's [Times Square](31389.md) to protest against the operation, with people chanting "Hands off Venezuela" and holding up signs that stated "US Out" and "No Blood for Oil".[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-wpix-3jan2026-42] Outside of New York, hundreds of people in different cities gathered to voice opposition to the strikes.[^cite_note-424]

One month after the strikes, the Venezuelan government called for a protest that gathered thousands of supporters for the release of Nicolás Maduro. His son [Nicolás Maduro Guerra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Maduro_Guerra) addressed the crowd, saying that the 3 January "will remain marked like a scar on our face, forever."[^cite_note-425]

### Financial markets and housing

Oil prices did not experience a surge, which had been normal with previous conflicts in oil-producing regions; prices increased 1% as of 5 January 2026. The reason is believed to be that Venezuela only represents 1% of the global oil supply and high oil production by other producers, including the United States, Saudi Arabia, and Brazil. The stock prices of companies that could become involved in Venezuela without spending money, such as oilfield service companies [SLB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schlumberger) and [Halliburton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halliburton), and refiners [Valero Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valero_Energy) and [Marathon Petroleum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marathon_Petroleum), surged on 5 January 2026. Some oil companies also saw increases, such as [ExxonMobil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ExxonMobil) and [ConocoPhillips](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ConocoPhillips).[^cite_note-426][^cite_note-427] Bonds issued by the Venezuelan government and the state oil company [PDVSA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PDVSA) surged by 30%. Venezuela's bonds went into default in 2017 and doubled in price during the escalating tensions in 2025.[^cite_note-428]

Housing prices in Caracas rose from 20% to 50%. In touristic sites like [Margarita Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Margarita_Island) the housing prices rose up to 80%. Some listing were paused waiting for a stabilization of the economy.[^cite_note-429]

### Polling

In October, before the strikes, a poll by [AtlasIntel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AtlasIntel) showed that approximately 64% of Venezuelans abroad support US military intervention to depose Maduro, compared with 34% within Venezuela.[^cite_note-430] In December, a poll by Datanalisis found that 55% of Venezuelans opposed foreign military intervention while 23% supported it.[^cite_note-431]

After the strikes, a poll by Reuters surveying 1,248 adults in the [United States](3434750.md) found that 33% of Americans approved of the military action, while 34% did not; 65% of Republicans supported the action compared to 11% of Democrats, and 72% of Americans were concerned that the US would get too involved in [Venezuela](32374.md), including 54% of Republicans.[^cite_note-reuterspoll-432] The same poll showed that Trump approval ratings rose to 42% after the attack, the highest rating since October 2025, when his approval rating was 39%.[^cite_note-reuterspoll-432]

A poll was made by *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* on 9 January, surveying 600 Venezuelan residents of different ages and sex to reflect the national population. Most respondents were positive about the intervention, only 13% of respondents mildly opposed the capture of Maduro. The 18% of respondents oppose United States governance in Venezuela. Most respondents want a quick change of government, with 43% supporting [María Corina Machado](5791389.md) as the leader and 13% supporting Delcy Rodríguez. The 91% of respondents answering in support for a change of government within a year.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434]

According to the *[Financial Times](136566.md)*, a poll on 30 January by Gold Glove Consulting, a firm run by Mark Feierstein, former Latin America adviser to US president Barack Obama, found that 72% of those polled considered that Venezuela was moving in a positive direction after Maduro's capture. In comparison, in June 2024, three-quarters of respondents thought the country was going in negative direction. 58% said security had worsened since January. In case of elections, 67% favored Maria Corina Machado against 25% for Delcy Rodríguez. As for Rodríguez government actions so far, 37% thought was good or excellent, while 22% did not know and 41% said it was bad or very bad.[^cite_note-435]

## Analysis

### Political

[International Crisis Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Crisis_Group) (ICG) analyst Elizabeth Dickinson disputed the success of the operation, noting that the Maduro regime appeared largely intact, with figures characterized as "hardliners" by Dickinson, such as [Diosdado Cabello](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diosdado_Cabello), occupying key posts such as the ministries of [defense](3003969.md) and [the interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Interior,_Justice_and_Peace_\(Venezuela\)). Dickinson concluded that "removing Maduro did not necessarily change the fundamental equation of control", adding that the government has withstood [sanctions](60408367.md) by rewarding its allies with access to scarce resources.[^cite_note-FortuneMag-436] Another ICG analyst, Phil Gunson cautioned anti-Maduro forces among the Venezuelan diaspora, reporting from [Caracas](55289.md) that "There's no dancing in the streets here".[^cite_note-WLRN-1-437] Months prior, Trump had previously denied seeking a change of power in [Venezuela](32374.md), saying he was targeting regime-aligned cartels rather than Maduro himself.[^cite_note-TimesPower-438] Trump's evident willingness to work with Rodríguez was criticized by Venezuelan opposition sectors.[^cite_note-NecessaryCNN-222]

Carrie Filipetti, member of the [Vandenberg Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vandenberg_Coalition) think tank and former [State Department](31975.md) official during the first Trump administration, believes that the US should focus on "turn\[ing] back to the democratically-elected leadership" in Venezuela to ensure both American and Venezuelan security. She explains that the [Venezuelan opposition](75340539.md), led by [Maria Corina Machado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Corina_Machado), "have the inroads" and "connections to the military" to create a "successful, smooth transition" and make sure "this doesn't devolve into chaos".[^cite_note-439] [Center for Strategic and International Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Strategic_and_International_Studies) (CSIS) specialist Clayton Seigle said oil companies sought stability in Venezuela above all else, adding that the outlook for US interests in the country remained uncertain. Another CSIS expert, Christopher Hernandez-Roy, said Trump's reluctance to engage in full regime change was driven by fear of chaos, noting that, aside from Maduro's removal, the current regime remained unchanged.[^cite_note-SameRegime-440]

American political analyst [David Rothkopf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Rothkopf) described President Trump's conduct as the "[Putinization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Putinization) of [US foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_foreign_policy)".[^cite_note-Borger-2026-441] Analysts Ryan C. Berg and Alexander B. Gray stated that the action showed a robust commitment to Trump's [2025 National Security Strategy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_National_Security_Strategy_\(United_States\)) (NSS) and a desire to increase preeminence in the Western Hemisphere, in turn signaling a "global reestablishment of deterrence" to countries such as China, alluding to the latter's investments in Venezuela. Atlantic Council Counterterrorism Project head Alex Plitsas urged an orderly transition, warning that instability in Venezuela could lead to political fragmentation and criminal violence involving colectivo gangs; Berg elaborated that any systemic change would likely be lengthy, while reinforcing the Trump administration's "[America First](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/America_First)" agenda.[^cite_note-442][^cite_note-443]

Indian political analyst [Brahma Chellaney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brahma_Chellaney) argued that while the legal framing (drug trafficking charges) resembles the [1989 invasion of Panama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_invasion_of_Panama) and the seizure of [Manuel Noriega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Noriega), the actual methodology and strategic narrative align more closely with the [2003 invasion of Iraq](201936.md).[^cite_note-444] [Douglas Farah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_Farah), a national security consultant and president of IBI Consultants, compared the US invasion of Panama to the current geopolitical situation in Venezuela, warning that although the [Panama](22997.md) operation was a relatively swift military victory, occupying Venezuela would present significantly greater challenges.[^cite_note-445] Professor Sultan Barakat, an expert from Qatar's [Hamad Bin Khalifa University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamad_Bin_Khalifa_University), argued that the US actions in Venezuela have set a dangerous precedent that could influence China's approach to [Taiwan](25734.md). He also warned that this precedent could potentially encourage Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) to adopt a more aggressive stance toward Ukrainian president [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](59210523.md).[^cite_note-446]

The *Washington Post* wrote that the lack of transparency and separation between business and diplomacy in the Trump administration's dictation of policy to the Venezuelan government has "raised questions about oversight in the affairs of the resource-rich nation that is emerging as a U.S. [neo-colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neocolonialism)."[^cite_note-\:6-51]

### Legality

| Re: Proposed War Department Operation to Support Law Enforcement Efforts in Venezuela |  |
| --- | --- |
| Seal of the United States Department of Justice |  |
| Created | 23 December 2025 |
| Signatories | [T. Elliot Gaiser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T._Elliot_Gaiser) |
| Subject | Legality under US law |
| Purpose | [Office of Legal Counsel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_Legal_Counsel) (OLC) Memo |
| Official website |  |
| [Memo](https://www.documentcloud.org/documents/26471613-olc-memo-on-venezuela-maduro-capture/) via [DocumentCloud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DocumentCloud) |  |

International law experts argued that the operation may have violated international law.[^cite_note-447] On 3 January 2026, [United Nations](31769.md) Spokesperson [Stéphane Dujarric](10302459.md) issued a statement that said the UN Secretary-General [António Guterres](471806.md) was "deeply alarmed by the recent escalation in Venezuela" and that United States' military action "has potential worrying implications for the region".[^cite_note-United_Nations_News-2026-378] Lawyer [Geoffrey Robertson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoffrey_Robertson) stated that the strike on Venezuela was contrary to [Article 2(4) of the UN Charter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_2\(4\)_of_the_UN_Charter) and qualifies as a [crime of aggression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_of_aggression) under international law. Elvira Domínguez-Redondo, professor of international law at [Kingston University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingston_University), also stated the strike was an act of aggression and that it could only have been lawful if it had authorization by a [United Nations Security Council resolution](178970.md), or was justified as "self-defense", but she added that there was "no evidence whatsoever" to support either condition.[^cite_note-McKelvie_Guardian-39]

[Ben Saul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben_Saul), a UN special [rapporteur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapporteur) and professor of international law, condemned "the US's illegal aggression against Venezuela and the illegal abduction of its leader and his wife" and called on Trump to be investigated and impeached.[^cite_note-saul-448][^cite_note-saul2-449] Professor of international law [Michael N. Schmitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_N._Schmitt), law professor [Ryan Goodman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ryan_Goodman) and Tess Bridgeman concluded that the operation against Venezuela "amounts to a severe breach of foundational principles of international law".[^cite_note-u528-450] Professor of law Ziyad Motala described the "intervention in Venezuela to abduct President Nicolás Maduro" as "international vandalism, plain and unadorned".[^cite_note-Motala-451] [UN experts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_special_rapporteur) described Trump's actions as constituting an "international crime of aggression", adding that these actions "represent a grave, manifest and deliberate violation of the most fundamental principles of international law, set a dangerous precedent, and risk destabilizing the entire region and the world".[^cite_note-UNE-452][^cite_note-UNECAAV-453]

The [International Commission of Jurists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Commission_of_Jurists) (ICJ) stated that US attacks against Venezuela and the abduction of President Nicolás Maduro violate international law and the UN Charter, and warned against interference in Venezuela's political future.[^cite_note-454]

### Oil infrastructure



Venezuela's [hyperinflation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperinflation_in_Venezuela) in the mid-2010s negatively impacted revenue from oil exports which magnified the decline of the industry.

Experts estimated that some 303 billion barrels of oil, or about 17% of the world's reserves, were located in Venezuela.[^cite_note-455][^cite_note-456] Much of it is [ultra-heavy crude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heavy_crude_oil), a [dense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Density), [viscous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viscosity), and high-sulfur type likened to a "semi-solid tar".[^cite_note-Reuters2026c-457][^cite_note-458][^cite_note-Rapier2026-459] Many US refineries along the [Gulf Coast](11969.md) process this type of crude oil from Canada and Mexico to make [diesel](81761.md), [jet fuel](1205435.md), [asphalt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asphalt_concrete), and elements of [petrochemicals](67728.md).[^cite_note-460][^cite_note-Ambrose2026-461][^cite_note-462][^cite_note-463]

Venezuela's oil industry had been in decline since [Hugo Chávez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Ch%C3%A1vez)'s wave of [expropriations](144089.md),[^cite_note-Rapier2026-459][^cite_note-Monaldi2021-464]<sup>: 2–3 </sup>[^cite_note-465] during which former oil contracts were shifted to [joint ventures](538782.md) with the state-owned [Petróleos de Venezuela S.A.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PDVSA) (PDVSA) and new [fiscal rules](132645.md) were imposed.[^cite_note-Reuters2026c-457][^cite_note-Monaldi2021-464]<sup>: 2, 4–5 </sup>[^cite_note-Rapier2017-466] Legal instability and other risks drove away investors, which Venezuela could not compensate for because of its overreliance on PDVSA's "inefficient operations, economic instability, discretionary policies, and poor investor protection",[^cite_note-Monaldi2021-464]<sup>: 8–9 </sup>[^cite_note-Rapier2017-466] worsened by US sanctions on the company since 2017.[^cite_note-Monaldi2021-464]<sup>: 7 </sup> In this time, the industry's oil infrastructure declined,[^cite_note-Reuters2026c-457][^cite_note-Rapier2026-459][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECrooksHamptonSomasekhar2026-467] and China increased cheap imports from Venezuela.[^cite_note-Stevenson2026-468]

Analysts, experts, and oil industry officials believed reconstruction could cost billions of dollars and take two to seven years or more,[^cite_note-471] requiring risky investment and a lengthy process of creating contracts.[^cite_note-475] Experts also said that US companies' refining capability could threaten China's presence in the Venezuelan oil industry.[^cite_note-Ambrose2026-461][^cite_note-Stevenson2026-468] Larger oil companies are reportedly less interested in investing in Venezuela due to high investment costs and the country's political instability, while the smaller companies that are most interested have been described by industry officials as the "least prepared and least sophisticated" firms to do so.[^cite_note-476][^cite_note-Reuters_1-9-2026-97][^cite_note-477]
